| INV-012 | Investigate: content/reference.aspose.org/en/3d/python/BoundingBoxExtent.md documents phantom __init__/extent_x/extent_y/extent_z members | 2026-08-01 | Surfaced by truth_audit.py 3d python during 3D-PYTHON-REFRESH-001 (5 FAIL: 4 `evidence.apis` unknown-member citations + 1 PHANTOM_METHOD on `__init__`). Confirmed pre-existing: content_hash on the page is unchanged across this entire refresh (the file was reverted, not touched), and the real upstream source (`runs/.clone_cache/aspose_3d_python/aspose/threed/utilities/BoundingBoxExtent.py`) shows `BoundingBoxExtent` is actually an enum-like class with `NULL`/`FINITE`/`INFINITE` class-level singleton values — no `__init__(extent_x, extent_y, extent_z)` constructor and no `extent_x`/`extent_y`/`extent_z` properties exist at all. The documented constructor/properties describe a completely different (vector/dimension-like) shape than the real class. `batch_reference.py --rebuild-all` did not regenerate this page during the refresh (0 candidates found — the run's citation-based impact scoring didn't flag it, since its own stale evidence.apis already "cited" the wrong members, so no delta was detected against them). Needs either a scoped `batch_reference.py 3d python --files BoundingBoxExtent.md` regeneration (once a way to force-target one file's rebuild exists) or a manual S-73 full-remediation rewrite grounded in the real source, following the same enum-singleton pattern already fixed on `ColladaTransformStyle` in this same refresh (see api_surface.py `_resolve_python_shim_collisions`, though that fix does not apply here — this isn't a name collision, just stale/wrong content that predates any scout defect this session found). resolved:2026-08-21 — root cause traced one level deeper: 3 enriched claims in `claims.json` (`ERC-3d-python-5d5a953e`/`-66535fad`/`-d3c0504c`, `claim_source:llm_member_doc`, confidence 0.7-0.8) fabricated `extent_x`/`extent_y`/`extent_z` as properties, contradicting the scout-verified `api_surface.json` entry in the same bundle — likely confused with the unrelated `BoundingBox.extent` property. Confirmed isolated: `audit.py` across all 120 3d/python reference pages found this exact failure pattern only in this one file (Token.md/TokenType.md have an unrelated, pre-existing stale-evidence issue on already-retired draft pages). Re-checked the `ColladaTransformStyle` precedent this note cites: it is NOT a rich enum-values fix — it's a minimal Overview-only page (matches 37 other sibling zero-method/zero-property enum-shaped classes found in 3d/python's `api_surface.json`), which is the current, consistent, passing house style; operator confirmed matching that style over adding a novel Values section. Fix: S-73 full-remediation on the content page (matching sibling house style) plus removing the 3 bad claim IDs from both `knowledge/3d/python/merged/claims.json` and `knowledge/3d/python/scout/enriched_claims.json` (durability — `promote.py` copies scout's claims additively with no dedup, so a merged-only removal would be resurrected on the next knowledge refresh). Follow-up filed separately (not actioned here): `batch_reference.py`'s enum classifier doesn't recognize the `bases: []` shape, so none of the 38 sibling classes get enum-member documentation even when correctly regenerated — a systemic completeness gap. CLOSED 2026-08-21 — content fix committed `6de10a2b6f` (audit.py 0 FAIL/0 WARN, verified against the full 154-file 3d/python platform audit with no regressions), knowledge-artifact fix (claim removal, superseded by a full re-scout/re-enrich/re-promote triggered as a side effect — see below) landed in `309ba30ad9`. Two tooling defects found and filed separately during the fix, not actioned here: (1) S-73's own step ordering runs `audit.py` before `attach_evidence.py`, so any full-remediation edit that removes API references from a page body cannot pass its own gate until evidence is refreshed out of documented order; (2) `scripts/pipeline/evidence/writer.py:_write_evidence_to_frontmatter`'s existing-apis prune only drops a stale reference when its whole class disappears from the knowledge model, not when a specific member of a still-valid class becomes invalid — `attach_evidence.py --force` could not remove the phantom `.__init__`/`.extent_x/y/z` member references even after the body no longer mentioned them; required a direct frontmatter correction. Also notable: `/manual-edit`'s own Step 5 knowledge-freshness check triggered a full 3d/python re-scout+enrich+promote (extractor hash had changed) as an unplanned side effect, which empirically confirmed the root-cause hypothesis — the fresh LLM re-enrichment did not re-hallucinate `extent_x/y/z` on this now-correctly-classified enum-shaped class. |

| INV-013 | Investigate: git commit-tree race-condition recovery (during 3D-PYTHON-REFRESH-001) transiently reverted a concurrent session's unrelated fix in the shared working tree | 2026-08-01 | During ST-021-style concurrent-session git-index contention while committing 3D-PYTHON-REFRESH-001's evidence-cleanup change, a `git update-ref refs/heads/main` was silently overwritten by a concurrent session's own commit (0e211de074, "fix(pipeline): fetch_aspose_com_targets.py silently accepted path-changing redirects as canonical"). Recovery was done via `git read-tree HEAD` + re-add the 7 intended 3d/python content paths + fresh `commit-tree`/`update-ref`, landing as d93192d9d201b0112f9fc1a14b6b53d416e62a80. Post-hoc verification (this session, during TC-05 closeout) found the recovery commit's tree was NOT actually rebuilt from the live HEAD at commit-tree time — its diff against its own parent (0e211de074) shows `data/aspose_com_targets.json`, `scripts/pipeline/commands/ops/fetch_aspose_com_targets.py`, and `scripts/pipeline/tests/test_fetch_aspose_com_targets.py` reverted back to their PRE-0e211de074 state (the `_redirect_changed_path`/`resolved_url` redirect-tracking fix and its 4 tests silently disappeared, and `cells/go`/`pdf/go` regressed to the wrong bundled-URL/404 entries in the registry) — likely a TOCTOU race where the temp index was read-tree'd from a HEAD reference captured before 0e211de074 landed, not re-read at actual commit-tree time. As of this investigation, the correct (0e211de074) content for all 3 files is already re-staged in the shared index (`git diff --cached` against 0e211de074 shows zero diff) — the owning session appears to have already noticed and restaged its fix; nothing was committed over it and none of this was ever pushed to origin. No action taken on those 3 files by this session (owning session's in-flight staged fix was left untouched, per CLAUDE.md ST-021 guidance against touching another session's live state). Recommendation: the isolated-temp-index git-plumbing recipe in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md should capture HEAD via a single atomic read immediately before `read-tree`, not reuse an earlier-captured ref — worth a hardening pass on that documented recipe itself. resolved:2026-08-01 — self-healed by the owning concurrent session before this session's TC-05 closeout; no residual corruption in tracked history or push queue. Filed for the paper trail and the recipe-hardening recommendation, not for further remediation. |

| INV-021 | Investigate: launch pipeline's blog-eligibility gate (`total_claims>=200 and snippet_count>=5`) can suppress the mandatory release-announcement post entirely — at least one blog post should be required regardless of claim count | 2026-09-12 | User directive: every launched FOSS product must get at least one blog post announcing the release, independent of how many claims/snippets its knowledge model has. Root mechanism located in `scripts/pipeline/commands/launch/site_planner.py::plan_blog()` (lines ~1263-1288): `justified = (total_claims >= 200 and snippet_count >= 5) or force_justify`, and `if not justified: return [], False, []` — this is an all-or-nothing gate on the *entire* blog section, including the mandatory `blog_role: "announcement"` intro post (weight 1, the one this directive is about), not just the optional `features`/`deep-dive` posts. A product whose knowledge model falls under the threshold currently gets **zero** blog pages, not a reduced set — no release announcement at all — unless an operator manually passes `--force-blog` (`force_justify`, explicitly documented as a human-only, never-automatic override). Not yet scoped: (a) how many already-launched products currently have zero blog pages because of this gate (needs a portfolio check — cross-reference `data/products.json`/launched families against `content/blog.aspose.org/{family}/{platform}/` presence); (b) whether the fix is to split `plan_blog()`'s gate so the announcement post alone bypasses the total_claims/snippet_count threshold while features/deep-dive posts remain gated, or something else. Not fixed here — user asked to backlog for later investigation and remediation. |

Not investigated or fixed here (out of scope for the marker-removal pass): needs checking whether this is a translation-introduced publish-status claim that contradicts `data/package_registry.json` (matching the `feedback_readme_no_publication_disclosure` rule: Installation-adjacent sections must never disclose publication status), or a real registry mismatch. The English canonical page should be checked for the same defect too. **Root-caused 2026-09-24**: genuine false positive, same mechanism as ST-156. `install_recipe.py`'s `_verify_publish_channel_claims` (`IR_PUBLISH_CLAIM`) scans for the bare word "npm" and suppresses the finding only when the same/previous line matches `_NOT_PUBLISHED_NEGATION_RE` — an English-literal regex (`not\s+(?:yet\s+)?(?:published\|available)\|coming\s+soon\|build(?:ing)?\s+from\s+source`). English passes because its sentence contains the literal phrase "build from source"; the Arabic translation of the SAME sentence (a faithful rendering: "...has a single runtime dependency: `xmldom`, ... installed automatically by npm") triggers the bare-"npm" match but can never match the English-only negation regex, regardless of what the Arabic text actually says. The Arabic sentence never claims npm publication — it only describes a build-time dependency fetch. Given this blind spot exists, the true count of this defect class across the full portfolio (English AND the much larger locale set already flagged in ST-211) is unknown and likely higher than either sweep reported. Needs: (1) fix the classifier so a swallowed-fence block with a blank info string is still checked against a REASONABLE default/inferred language (or at minimum flagged as its own distinct, non-silent category rather than folded into the same generic `GRAMMAR-UNAVAILABLE` bucket as a legitimately-unsupported language); (2) re-run the full portfolio sweep with the fix and reconcile against the original 44/2290 counts. **RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (TC-20, MT072)**: re-verified the specific mechanism live -- it does NOT hold under current code. `registry.NON_EXECUTABLE_LANGS` already contains `""` alongside real declared non-code tags (yaml/json/css/...), so a blank-info-string block was already landing in the real, disclosed `NOT-EXECUTABLE-LANG` bucket, never silently masquerading as checked-and-clean in `GRAMMAR-UNAVAILABLE`. The genuine problem was merging blank-lang blocks into the SAME bucket as deliberate non-code tags, hiding the signal: 34 such blocks existed in the current English portfolio, invisible inside `NOT-EXECUTABLE-LANG`'s combined total of 71. Fixed: blank-lang blocks now get their own `BLANK-LANG-UNCHECKED` bucket. Reconciled full re-run: 3,858 blocks total, still 0 unclassified; `NOT-EXECUTABLE-LANG` 71->37, `BLANK-LANG-UNCHECKED` (new) 33. Separately found+fixed live while doing this: `example_verification_sweep.py` and its own test had never actually been committed to git in this mission's ~10 prior commits despite TC-09 closing on it -- committed for the first time, registered in registry.yaml, added to the CI workflow's file list (was omitted from TC-12's own list for the same reason). | ST-102 | SYS | GI | `scripts/ci/hooks/repair_venv.sh` line 21 has an operator-precedence bug: `REPO_ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../../.." && pwd)"`. Because bash parses `A \|\| B && C` as `(A \|\| B) && C`, when `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (A) succeeds, `pwd` (C) still runs unconditionally afterward and its output is appended into the same command substitution, giving REPO_ROOT an embedded newline (both paths were identical in the reproducing case, so the printed value showed the same path twice on two lines). `VENV_DIR="$REPO_ROOT/.venv"` then inherits the embedded newline, and `python -m venv "$VENV_DIR"` fails with `[WinError 123] The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect`. Reproduced live 2026-08-29 in a fresh worktree with no `.venv`: `bash scripts/ci/hooks/repair_venv.sh` printed a duplicated REPO_ROOT/VENV_DIR (one path, then the same path again on the next line) and failed venv creation; worked around by running `python -m venv .venv` directly per the script's own documented manual fallback instead of using the script. Fix: wrap the fallback in parens — `git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null \|\| (cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../../.." && pwd)` — so `pwd` only runs when `git rev-parse` actually fails. | queued | P1 | XS | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-29 |

| ST-118 | SYS | KF | `scripts/pipeline/extraction/snippets.py`'s code-extraction paths (4 call sites: lines 496, 562, 622, 682, plus a 5th at ~977 in the tiered-budget renumbering pass) blindly truncate an extracted function/method body to `code[:5000]` — a raw Python character-count slice with zero awareness of token, statement, or brace boundaries. When a real source function exceeds 5000 characters, the cap slices it off mid-token, producing syntactically invalid, unbalanced code. Confirmed live 2026-08-30 (MT040, TC-MT040-32 quality gates, investigating `launch_gate.py`'s L-15 WARN "2 snippet issue(s) out of 101 — bracket imbalance"): `knowledge/pdf/typescript/merged/snippets/snippet_011.ts` (150 open / 145 close braces per the checker; 38/34 by direct recount against the file's *current* content) ends literally mid-identifier — `hexdump` confirms the file's last bytes are the truncated word `verts` (from `const verts: number[] = [];` two lines earlier and a `for` loop body never closed), 4 open braces (function body, array literal, 2 nested object/arrow-function blocks) with zero matching closes. This is not pdf/typescript-specific or TypeScript-specific — the identical `code[:5000]` pattern is repeated across every language's extraction path in the same file, so any sufficiently large function in ANY product/platform can produce the same corruption. WARN-only (`launch_gate.py`'s L-15), not currently blocking any launch — `member_validity`/`code_block_api`/other content-eval evaluators check API-reference validity against `api_surface.json`, not raw code-block syntactic completeness, so a truncated snippet doesn't itself fail those gates unless/until an agent cites it verbatim in generated content. Not fixed here: a proper fix needs a decision (truncate at the nearest safe statement/line boundary with an explicit `// ...truncated` marker, vs. exclude the whole snippet from the candidate pool when its natural body exceeds the cap) applied consistently across all ~5 call sites and all languages — a cross-cutting extraction change out of scope for a single-platform launch. Filed so the real defect is tracked rather than silently absorbed as "just a WARN." | queued | P3 | M | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-30 |

| ST-126 | SYS | GI | `git_plumb_commit.py` (the mandated, sole sanctioned way to commit under CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md) has no flag to attach the `Refresh-Run`/`Refresh-Base`/`Refresh-Source-SHA` git trailers `refresh_run.py bind_commit()` requires (checked via `_commit_trailer_values()` against `git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r <commit>` of that SINGLE commit's own diff, not the cumulative base..commit range) -- so any session following the documented `refresh_run --mode combined` procedure (start -> run -> verify -> commit via git_plumb_commit.py -> bind-commit) and NOT separately hand-crafting those 3 trailer lines into the commit message will always hit `bind-commit`'s trailer-missing BLOCKED error after the real content commit is already pushed, with no clean recovery: amending a published commit is forbidden (CLAUDE.md Git Safety Protocol), and `bind_commit()`'s own diff-tree check requires the SAME commit that carries the trailers to also be the one with real changed paths -- a separate empty/marker follow-up commit is explicitly rejected ("has no changed paths"), so there is no forward-fix path once the real commit has already landed without trailers. The real content is untouched, exactly where `git_plumb_commit.py` already placed it; the shadow commit exists purely to satisfy the refresh_run governance contract and is pushed to a dedicated non-branch ref (`--push-ref`) for durable, inspectable provenance. | ST-145 | SYS | GI | `cross_platform_audit.py`'s `compare_family()` built each platform's format→direction map with a plain dict comprehension keyed only by lowercased format name, iterating over the RAW formats.json entry list -- when a format has multiple entries for the same platform (a common, correct pattern: separate `*Importer.ts`/`*Exporter.ts` files, or separate `LoadOptions`/`SaveOptions` classes, each contributing its own "import" or "export" token), only whichever entry happened to be LAST in file order survived; every earlier entry for that key was silently overwritten, never aggregated. Confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT040 Phase I, TC-MT040-56) via `readiness_gate.py 3d typescript`'s G-07 (Cross-platform consistency) BLOCKER: it reported `fbx`/`gltf`/`obj` as "typescript=import" only, contradicting `formats.json` itself (independently verified: all 3 formats have BOTH an `Importer.ts` entry with `direction: import` AND an `Exporter.ts`/`SaveOptions.ts` entry with `direction: export` -- confirmed both directly and via the FbxLoadOptions/FbxSaveOptions classes' own documented existence). Pre-existing since the file's creation (commit `f7a71f79e6`, 2026-04-29), untouched until this fix -- not something introduced this session. Confirmed to affect every family with per-direction-file format entries, not just 3d: live before/after comparison on `cells`, `pdf`, and `words` all showed the same last-write-wins collapse pattern (e.g. pdf's `docx: net=import, python=export` was almost certainly both platforms supporting both directions, collapsed to whichever entry loaded last). Root cause and fix: rewrote the platform-format map builder to aggregate ALL direction/support tokens seen per (platform, format) into a set first, then normalize -- `{"import","export"}` (in any order, with or without a pre-existing literal `"both"` token, which some extractors already write directly for a single class handling both directions, e.g. java's `Workbook.java` XLSX entry) collapses to `"both"`; a single remaining token passes through unchanged (preserving all currently-correct single-direction comparisons, e.g. `enum_only`). 3 new regression tests added (`test_same_platform_multiple_entries_aggregate_not_last_write_wins`, `test_genuine_cross_platform_mismatch_still_detected_after_aggregation`, plus the pre-existing single-entry test unmodified and still passing) proving the aggregation both resolves the false positive AND still detects a genuinely real cross-platform mismatch. Live re-verification after the fix: 3d/typescript's fbx/obj/collada/gltf entries are now fully consistent with java/net (0 typescript-specific discrepancies remaining); a separate, unrelated, pre-existing "detect" direction token present only in python's 3d formats.json (not typescript's, not introduced by this fix) accounts for the family's 8 remaining `cross_platform_audit` issues -- out of scope here (predates this session, affects python vs java/net only, unrelated to the typescript remediation this mission covers). | resolved | P1 | S | 0 | none | related-to:TC-MT040-56 | 2026-08-31 | **resolved:2026-08-31** -- fixed the dict-comprehension aggregation bug in `compare_family()` (`scripts/pipeline/commands/content/cross_platform_audit.py`); 3 regression tests pass (`test_cross_platform_unittest.py`); live-verified fbx/obj/gltf/collada now correctly show typescript=both matching java/net, resolving the false G-07 BLOCKER for 3d/typescript. The residual python-`detect`-token family-wide discrepancy (unrelated to typescript) is left as pre-existing, out-of-scope state, not re-filed separately since it is a genuine (if minor) fleet-wide extraction-consistency question rather than a tool bug -- worth a future look if another mission touches 3d/python's format extraction. |

| ST-149 | SYS | KF | `content_eval`'s `api_completeness` (AC) evaluator flags a subclass reference page's inherited-but-redeclared properties as "not documented" when they are correctly documented on the BASE class's own reference page instead of being duplicated on every subclass page -- a pattern this evaluator has no awareness of. Confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT040 Phase I, TC-MT040-56): after `LoadOptions`/`SaveOptions` (2 of the 9 newly-covered previously-unreachable abstract classes from this same commit, ST-133 chain) gained their own reference pages for the first time, 12 concrete subclass pages (`{Collada,Fbx,Gltf,Obj,Stl,ThreeMf}{Load,Save}Options.md`) each dropped from grade A to C -- all 12 flagged by AC as "5/6 public members (83%) not documented: encoding, fileFormat, fileName, fileSystem, lookupPaths" (Load) or the Save-side equivalent. Verified NOT a real defect: `api_surface.json` records these 5 property names as `FbxLoadOptions`'s (and every sibling's) OWN properties (`bases: ['LoadOptions']`, own `properties` list includes all 5 inherited-and-redeclared names -- likely how the TS scout represents members visible on a subclass without re-declaring them, an extraction-shape question in its own right, not investigated further here), and `LoadOptions.md`'s own Properties table genuinely documents all 5 with real descriptions and types -- the documentation IS complete, just correctly split across base/subclass pages (avoiding 12x duplication) rather than repeated per page. Root cause: `api_completeness.py` checks a page's OWN Properties/Methods table against `api_surface.json`'s member list for that page's OWN class only, with no cross-page/base-class awareness -- same class of blind spot as ST-138 (`member_validity`'s inability to attribute a multi-CLASS page's sections to the right class), except here it is a multi-PAGE base/subclass split rather than a multi-class single page. Non-blocking: `check_grade_downgrade.py`'s Step 29 gate only WARNs on a grade drop of this size (does not FAIL/block), so the commit proceeded; the 12 pages now carry a real, evaluator-driven grade of C in frontmatter despite genuinely-complete documentation -- left as computed (not hand-overridden), matching this session's established precedent for evaluator-gap-driven grade caps (see the 2 grade-C pages already documented in `reports/publish-review/3d-typescript-20260831/final-verdict.md`'s "Grade-C Pages Below the Mission's Docs/KB Floor" section). Not fixed here -- teaching `api_completeness.py` to walk a class's `bases` chain and treat a member as "documented" if it appears on ANY ancestor class's own reference page (not just the current page) is a real, moderate-scope change to a widely-used evaluator affecting every product with a base/subclass reference-page split, not just 3d/typescript. Fix candidate: for each undocumented-member finding, before flagging, check whether the member name also appears in the nearest base class's OWN (non-inherited) `api_surface.json` properties/methods list; if so and that base class has its own generated reference page, suppress the finding (or downgrade it to INFO citing the base page). Likely affects any family/platform with an options-class or similar shallow-inheritance pattern once its base classes gain their own pages (a fleet-wide, not-yet-surveyed exposure). | resolved | P2 | M | 0 | related-to:TC-MT040-56 | none | 2026-08-31 | **resolved:2026-08-31** -- implemented the fix candidate exactly: before flagging a member as missing, `api_completeness.py` now walks the class's `bases` chain (cycle-safe), and for each ancestor whose OWN `properties`/`methods` list also contains the member name, looks for that ancestor's own reference page (`{Base}.md` sibling, falling back to a `{Base}-*.md` glob for the namespace/collision-suffixed naming convention) and checks whether the member is genuinely documented there (same table/header/backtick extraction the evaluator already uses on its own page). Only members actually found documented on an ancestor's page are suppressed; a genuinely undocumented member is still flagged even if it shares a name with something on a base class -- confirmed via a dedicated regression test where the base page is deliberately left incomplete. 4 new tests in `test_api_completeness_base_class_split.py` (satisfied-by-base suppression, still-flags-genuine-gaps, no-base-page-falls-back-to-old-behavior, unrelated-class-with-no-bases-unaffected); full content_eval scoped bundle (455 tests) passes with zero regressions. Live-verified against the exact production case: re-ran `api_completeness` on all 12 real affected pages (`{Collada,Fbx,Gltf,Obj,Stl,ThreeMf}{Load,Save}Options.md`) -- 0 findings (was 12 FAIL/WARN before the fix) -- then re-graded them via `content_eval --write-grade`: all 12 restored from the false-capped `grade: C` back to `grade: A`. |

Confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT040 Phase I, TC-MT040-56): 3d/typescript's `refresh_run --mode combined` (run `rf-20260831T033305Z-c8631942f779`) blocked `bind-commit` on 2 docs pages (`developer-guide/features.md`, `developer-guide/scene-graph.md`) whose npm-publish-wording fix had already landed in an earlier commit (`0d981cfb87`, part of the same session's IR/FM batch fix) before the run's 277-file consolidation commit (`1ace625a02`). Also hit, same session, same run, two EARLIER `bind_commit` checks with the identical root shape (a legitimate out-of-band write the run's own bookkeeping didn't anticipate): `_assert_scope_ownership`'s OCC guard (190 content files whose hash changed via a post-apply whole-product `content_eval --write-grade` re-grade sweep, not the run's own receipts) and `_allowed_commit_paths` (107 `knowledge/3d/typescript/merged/**` files, a re-sync the run itself produced but whose paths were never in the pinned content-only `scope.paths`) -- both resolved live via the EXISTING, already-sanctioned `_record_extra_allowed_hash` mechanism (191 total hashes registered across both categories), matching the ST-134 precedent exactly. | resolved | P1 | S | 0 | none | related-to:TC-MT040-56 | 2026-08-31 | **resolved:2026-08-31** -- fixed the exact-match check: a decision-row page missing from `changed_paths` is now exempted when it is registered in `extra_allowed_hashes` AND that registered hash matches the resolved commit's real git-tracked content (verified via the same `cat-file --filters` technique the later per-row hash check already uses) -- proving the write was both authorized and actually landed, not merely intended. This affected not just my new exemption but the EXISTING, older `hash_mismatches` per-row check's own `committed_raw_hash` comparison too (same root cause, second call site) -- rejecting `bind-commit` on 5 more pages with "Bound commit content does not match run decisions" even after the first fix landed. Fixed by extracting a shared `_committed_text_matches_disk()` helper (universal-newline TEXT comparison, matching the `\r\n`->`\n` normalization the surrounding code already used for its OTHER two hash checks) and applying it consistently to both call sites; the first fix's inline duplicate was replaced with a call to the shared helper. 1 more regression test (116 total in the suite). Commit `c95f9fd588`. **3d/typescript's `bind-commit` succeeded on the next retry -- run `rf-20260831T033305Z-c8631942f779` reached `COMMITTED`, `commit_sha=1ace625a02c505578d1b9e818e48960eebb5cdb4`.** This CRLF/`--filters` gap likely affects every future `bind_commit` call on any CRLF-committed page across the whole fleet, not just 3d/typescript -- both fixes are generic, not product-specific. |
| ST-151 | SYS | KF | 53 of 3d/typescript's 2611 locale files (`mesh.md` x26 locales, `scene.md` x27 locales) carry genuinely stale, orphaned prose sections that no longer correspond to ANY content on the current English page -- confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT040 Phase I, TC-MT040-57 locale handoff) via direct grep: the Spanish `mesh.md` locale documents `createElement`/`createElementUV` parameter examples using PascalCase enum member references (`VertexElementType.Normal`, `MappingMode.ControlPoint`, `ReferenceMode.Direct`, `TextureMapping.Diffuse`) that (a) don't exist anywhere in the current English `mesh.md` (zero grep matches) and (b) are factually wrong regardless -- the real enum member names are UPPER_SNAKE_CASE (`NORMAL`, `CONTROL_POINT`, `DIRECT`, `DIFFUSE`, confirmed against `api_surface.json`). The Spanish `scene.md` locale similarly documents a `buffer` parameter as "Un Node.js `Buffer` que contiene el contenido completo del archivo" with zero corresponding English content. Root cause: this session's `refresh_run --mode combined` (rf-20260831T033305Z-c8631942f779) applied a real S-105 body-refresh to both English pages (part of the same 277-file consolidation commit, `1ace625a02`) that evidently removed/restructured these sections, but the S-107 locale-handoff's literal-text-diff patching (`derive_patches` via `difflib.SequenceMatcher` on whole lines) cannot catch this class of change: a REMOVED section has no corresponding "new_chunk" to patch TO, and even where content survives, translated prose never contains the English "before" text verbatim, so line-level replace-patches never match inside a foreign-language sentence -- confirmed live: this same mechanism separately caused a genuine but recoverable miss (35 `features.md` locales retaining a stale, factually-wrong `npm install @aspose/3d` command already fixed in English this session -- that one WAS fixable by a targeted literal code-span replacement since the broken text was an untranslated inline code literal, not translated prose; hand-fixed directly, not via this backlog entry). `audit.py` correctly refuses `attach_evidence.py --force` on these 53 files (real content-accuracy FAILs, not stale-metadata WARNs) -- they remain evidence-stale, NOT silently re-stamped over broken content. Not fixed here -- correcting orphaned prose in 20+ languages requires either native-language translation capability this session does not have, or a structural "remove content that has no English counterpart" pass that risks corrupting locale file structure without careful per-file verification; both are out of scope for a content-diff-routing locale handoff (Decision 19 explicitly prohibits blanket re-translation). Fix candidates: (a) extend `locale_refresh_handoff.py`/S-107 to detect REMOVED sections (an S-105 receipt showing a body section deleted) and flag the corresponding locale sections for deletion rather than attempting a text patch that can never match; (b) a governed, human-reviewed (or S-90-routed) per-locale correction pass for these 2 specific pages' 53 files; (c) generalize the literal-code-span-replacement technique used for the `features.md` npm fix into a reusable S-75 sub-strategy for untranslated inline-code defects specifically, separate from the prose-removal case these 53 files actually need. **Scope correction, 2026-08-31 (re-investigated while working this row):** the true divergence is SUBSTANTIALLY LARGER than "2 specific orphaned examples" -- current English `mesh.md`/`scene.md` have ZERO `### ` method-detail subheadings at all (just a `## Methods` summary TABLE), while the Spanish locale alone carries 8 orphaned H3s in `mesh.md` (`createPolygon`, `triangulate`, `toMesh`, `static union`, `static difference`, `static intersect`, `createElement`, `createElementUV`) and 4 in `scene.md`, under a completely different H2 outline (three `## Examples` headings, no `## Overview`/`## Properties`/`## Methods`/`## See Also` at all vs English's exact 4-section structure) -- i.e. these locale pages were generated under an OLDER page-template format (detailed per-method H3 breakdown) that the current template has fully retired in favor of a summary table, and the divergence is architectural, not a couple of stale examples. A mechanical "delete the orphaned H3s" script is UNSAFE here: it would leave a structurally mismatched page (still missing English's `## Overview`/`## Properties`/`## Methods`/`## See Also` shape) rather than a corrected one, and the exact boundary/count differs per locale (verified only for `es` so far; the other ~26 `mesh.md` and ~27 `scene.md` locales are not individually confirmed to share the identical outline). This is now understood to be the SAME class of problem as ST-152 (product-wide structural/template drift), not a narrower, independently-fixable case -- **not attempted this session**: forcing a rushed structural rewrite across ~53 files in 20+ languages without real per-file verification is exactly the risk this row's own text already warned against. Recommend folding ST-151 into whatever dedicated remediation mission eventually addresses ST-152 (a full page-template regeneration pass per locale, likely via a future `refresh_run`-class mechanism once one exists for structural/template reconciliation, not text-diff patching) rather than treating it as a smaller, separately-closeable item. **2026-09-01 update**: promoted to Active (P1) with a real remediation plan — see `backlog/BACKLOG.md`, `plans/from_backlog/2026-09-01-st-151-st-152-3d-typescript-locale-drift-remediation.md`. Part A (S-107 RG-004 wiring) CLOSED, commit `6186b9768188c170b1cff51f75883402bfe996b7`, pushed. Part B (content regen) IN PROGRESS: batch-1 pilot (8 locales) found the real scope is 25 of 36 locales translatable via ht-translate (11 excluded by hugo-translator's own `strict_locale_allowlist`, tracked as a new follow-up TC-LOCALE-02-07) plus 2 locales (de, fr) needing a re-run without `--force-accept` due to real fidelity defects found by the tool's own self-check. | in-progress | P1 | M | 0 | plans/from_backlog/2026-09-01-st-151-st-152-3d-typescript-locale-drift-remediation.md | related-to:TC-MT040-57,ST-152 | 2026-09-01 |

| ST-153 | SYS | GI | `check_translation_regression.py`'s R3 check ("staged version has fewer code-fence lines than the previous version") has no scoped override for a legitimate, verified-correct removal -- confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT040 Phase I, TC-MT040-57 locale handoff): the Arabic locale of `docs/3d/typescript/developer-guide/features.md` correctly received an S-107/S-75 literal patch propagating the English page's own PRE-EXISTING `<!-- Example removed: constructor API no longer available -->` marker (already present in English at commit time, confirming the removal was intentional and upstream-verified, not something this session introduced) -- R3 flagged this as a "fence stripping" regression with no way to accept it short of `--skip-tier1-checks`, which disables all 38 Tier-1 checks for the whole commit batch, far too blunt for a 2559-file batch. Resolved by reverting just this one file (`git checkout --`) and excluding it from the commit -- it remains at its pre-session state (still carrying the stale, already-invalid code example) rather than either being force-committed under a blanket skip or silently left in a worse state. Not fixed here -- R3 needs either a per-file override mechanism (mirroring the EVIDENCE-RESTAMP commit-trailer pattern already used elsewhere in this repo for exactly this "legitimate exception, narrow scope" shape) or a smarter heuristic that tolerates a code-fence removal when the replacement text matches a literal marker ALREADY present in the current English source at the same relative position. Fix candidate: extend `git_plumb_commit.py`'s commit-message trailer convention with a `TRANSLATION-REGRESSION-ACCEPT: <path>` annotation, verified the same way EVIDENCE-RESTAMP is (citing evidence the removal is upstream-confirmed), so a single genuinely-correct file doesn't force either a full-batch skip or a manual exclude-and-refile cycle. | resolved | P3 | S | 0 | related-to:TC-MT040-57 | none | 2026-08-31 | **resolved:2026-08-31** -- implemented the "smarter heuristic" alternative (fully automatic, no manual trailer needed at all): `check_translation_regression.py` now derives the page's English counterpart path (`content/<site>/en/<rest>`) and, when the staged locale page's own post-edit fence count is still >= the English counterpart's CURRENT fence count, treats the reduction as catching up to a removal that already happened on English rather than new divergence -- a locale page that starts out below English's fence count and drops further still fails, unchanged. 9 new regression tests (`english_counterpart_path()` path-shape coverage; the catch-up-passes / still-below-English-fails / no-english-data-preserves-prior-behavior / shortcode-substitution-unaffected cases). Live-verified against the EXACT real case this finding describes: re-applied the Arabic `docs/3d/typescript/developer-guide/features.md` patch (reverted at the time this row was filed) via `/manual-edit` -- `check_translation_regression.py` now accepts it cleanly with no `--skip-tier1-checks` needed. While live-verifying, found and fixed 2 more real defects surfaced along the way (both filed as their own rows): a `content_eval` `Page._infer_metadata()` locale-parsing bug (ST-155) and ST-142 (3d/typescript ThreeMf format-detection false-negative, resolved same session, see its own row) plus a genuine extra untranslated-source sentence on that same Arabic page (fixed via `/manual-edit`, not a backlog row -- one-off content fix). |
| ST-154 | SYS | GI | ST-126's fix (`refresh_run_bind_recovery.py`, shadow-commit technique) unblocks a `refresh_run` that is ALREADY stuck at `READY_TO_COMMIT` with a trailer-less pushed commit; it is reactive recovery, not prevention -- `git_plumb_commit.py` still has no way to attach the 3 required `Refresh-Run`/`Refresh-Base`/`Refresh-Source-SHA` trailers at commit time when its `--files` happen to be a refresh_run's own scope, so the next session following the documented `start -> run -> verify -> commit -> bind-commit` procedure without hand-crafting the trailers will hit the identical BLOCKED state again (recoverable now, but avoidable). Fix candidate: the `--refresh-run {family} {platform} {run_id}` convenience flag on `git_plumb_commit.py` originally proposed in ST-126's fix-candidate note -- looks up the run's `base_commit_sha`/`target_source_sha` and appends the trailers automatically during the normal commit, so `bind-commit` never needs recovery in the first place. Found live 2026-08-31 while closing MT040 (TC-MT040-53); not fixed this session -- ST-126's own recovery tool was the higher-priority, already-triggered gap. | resolved | P3 | S | 0 | none | related-to:ST-126,TC-MT040-53 | 2026-08-31 | **resolved:2026-08-31** -- added a `--refresh-run {family} {platform} {run_id}` flag to `git_plumb_commit.py` (via a new `_stamp_refresh_run_trailers()` helper) that looks up the run's `base_commit_sha`/`target_source_sha` from its manifest and appends the 3 required trailers to the commit message before committing, idempotently (a message that already carries the exact correct trailers is left unchanged, so a retried commit build is never double-stamped). Errors cleanly (exit 2, no commit created) if the run hasn't reached `verify` yet (`target_source_sha` not pinned). 6 new regression tests in `test_git_plumb_commit_refresh_run_trailers.py`: 4 unit-level round-tripping the stamped message through `refresh_run._commit_trailer_values()` (the exact parser `bind_commit()` itself uses) plus idempotency and both error paths, and 2 CLI-level using a real temporary git repo (not mocked) proving the flag's end-to-end wiring through `main()` -- one happy path confirming the real committed HEAD carries all 3 correct trailers, one confirming a clean CLI error with no commit created when the run isn't verified yet. Full scoped bundle (git_plumb_commit + refresh_run suites, 198 tests) passes with zero regressions. **Follow-up 2026-09-10:** the file's own `test_cli_refresh_run_flag_stamps_trailers_on_the_real_commit` regression test was found failing (a later, unrelated 2026-08-31 commit made `--co-author`/`Skills invoked:` mandatory for every `git_plumb_commit.py` invocation, and this test's call predated that). Fixing the test's missing `--co-author` argument surfaced a REAL production regression, not just a stale fixture: `main()` called `_ensure_commit_provenance_trailers()` AFTER `_stamp_refresh_run_trailers()`, so the provenance trailers (Skills invoked / Co-Authored-By) were appended as a new paragraph on top of the Refresh-Run/Refresh-Base/Refresh-Source-SHA block. `refresh_run._commit_trailer_values()` — the function `bind_commit()` itself uses to verify a commit — only recognizes the message's FINAL trailer paragraph, so every `--refresh-run` commit built since the co-author mandate landed (2026-08-31) would have had its refresh-run trailers silently invisible to `bind-commit`. Fixed by swapping the order in `main()` so refresh-run trailers are always stamped last (commit `39bce100284f`). Scoped regression bundle (git_plumb_commit + encoding + reconcile + refresh-run-trailers, 87 tests) passes with zero regressions. |
| ST-155 | SYS | GI | `content_eval`'s `Page._infer_metadata()` (`content_eval/models.py`) only stripped the site-relative locale segment when it literally equaled `"en"` -- for every OTHER locale (ar, de, fr, ...) the locale code itself was misread as `family` and the real family misread as `platform` (e.g. `content/docs.aspose.org/ar/3d/typescript/...` -> `family="ar", platform="3d"`). Any DIRECT evaluation of a non-English page (`content_eval evaluate --files <locale-path>`, exactly what S-73/manual-edit's own post-edit re-grade step does) then hit the runner's `"Knowledge unavailable for {family}/{platform}"` PIPELINE-FAIL short-circuit, which SKIPS running every other evaluator entirely for that page (`runner.py` L159-177: `if knowledge and not available: ... continue`) -- so any grade ever written via this exact path for a non-English page was computed with zero real evaluators run, silently, and the runner's own code comment already warns "Grading without knowledge produces inflated grades." Found live 2026-08-31 while using `/manual-edit` on an Arabic 3d/typescript page (ST-153 verification): its pre-existing frontmatter said `grade: A`, but the FIRST real evaluation this page had ever received (after the fix) found 2 genuine, previously-invisible defects and computed `grade: B`. `discover_content()` (`lib/content_discovery.py`), the batch/production grading route used by fleet-wide truth-audit/S-90/`content_eval evaluate {family} {platform}`, only ever returns English paths (confirmed by its own docstring, "Find all English content .md files") -- so this bug did NOT affect fleet/product-level grading, only ad-hoc single-file locale evaluation. Scope of exposure not fully surveyed: any OTHER prior S-73/S-26/manual invocation that graded a non-English page directly may have recorded an equally untrustworthy grade; a fleet sweep of frontmatter `grade:` values on non-English pages cross-checked against a real re-evaluation would quantify this, not attempted here (out of scope for this finding). Root-caused and fixed: `_infer_metadata()` now reuses `content_discovery.py`'s own `LOCALE_CODES` set and its exact `remaining[0] == "en" or remaining[0] in LOCALE_CODES` check (the same ambiguity was ALREADY correctly solved once in this codebase, in `infer_product()`, just never reused here -- a second, buggy, independent implementation of the same logic). Added a `Page.locale` field (previously untracked). 6 new regression tests (`test_page_locale_metadata.py`): English/Arabic/German/reference-subdomain paths, blog's English-no-locale-prefix vs blog-translated-with-locale-prefix, products' single-segment shape, and an unrecognized-path-shape guard. | ST-158 | SYS | GI | Interrupting a `refresh_run.py` execution mid-flight during its knowledge-enrichment sub-step (e.g. killing a hung background `resume`/`run` call to switch LLM providers) can leave `page_decisions.jsonl` carrying a stale `body_hash_after` value from the FIRST (interrupted) content-generation pass, even though the actual on-disk/committed content correctly reflects a LATER, successful pass. Confirmed live 2026-08-31 (MT033-FANOUT-001 slice, pdf/go, run `rf-20260831T135742Z-3a638764fe43`): killed a background `resume --dry-run` that was enriching against a local Ollama fallback (professionalize.com was returning a genuine transient timeout at that moment) to retry with professionalize.com once it recovered; the retried run completed successfully, but its own `post_refresh_verify.py` Check 8 (Case A, RPR-J-05) then FAILED, reporting "205 of 205 page(s) recorded as BODY_UPDATED show NO real body diff" against git. Root-caused: `_normalized_body_hash` recomputed directly against the real committed before/after content confirms the pages are genuinely unchanged (matches `content_hash` being byte-identical in the frontmatter) -- the DECISION RECORD's `body_hash_after` field is simply stale, not the actual content. `abandon`'s own built-in `_restore_scope_to_baseline` safety mechanism correctly refused to auto-restore ("Recovery refused because a scoped path no longer matches the run output or pinned input"), leaving the operator to manually `git checkout --`/remove the dirty working tree by hand before restarting. Not fixed here. **Correction (2026-08-31, independent review):** the originally-stated root-cause hypothesis below does NOT hold up -- `_receipt_already_applied()` (batch_reference.py L2449-2457) already re-verifies the CURRENT on-disk file's own sha256 hash against the receipt's `new_hash` before reusing it, so it is not blindly trusting stale first-pass state. The TRUE mechanism producing the `body_hash_after` mismatch is still unidentified; the symptom (real, reproduced) and the safe recovery path (abandon + manual working-tree revert) remain accurate and are the load-bearing part of this finding. Original (now-superseded) hypothesis, kept for the record rather than deleted: ~~the likely mechanism is `_receipt_already_applied()`'s "receipt already applied, reuse recorded verdict" shortcut trusting a receipt written during the FIRST, interrupted pass without re-verifying it against the SECOND pass's actual output~~. Investigating the real mechanism, and/or having `resume` proactively clear any staging/receipt state from a prior incomplete attempt before proceeding, is real, moderate-scope pipeline hardening out of scope for this finding. Fix candidates: (a) `_receipt_already_applied()` re-verifies the cached receipt's `body_hash_after` against the CURRENT staged content before trusting it, not just its existence; (b) `resume` documents (or enforces) that a run interrupted mid-generation-pass should not be resumed without first discarding any partial staging/receipt state from the interrupted attempt; (c) `abandon`'s refusal message could name the exact taskcard-style recovery steps (checkout tracked files, rm untracked new pages) rather than leaving the operator to derive them live, as this session had to. **Second correction (2026-09-01, mission MT060's line-level reassessment supersedes both hypotheses above):** the TRUE mechanism is RC-8 in `plans/from_backlog/2026-08-31-mt060-refresh-prose-capability.md` — `batch_reference.py`'s write path order is generate → stage → `attach_evidence(force=True)` → `_record_reference_attempt()` emits the `BODY_UPDATED` decision BEFORE the real write → `detect_backlink_downgrade()` raises (the regenerated candidate lost the page's existing qualifying aspose.com backlink) → a per-entry `except` swallows it → `safe_content_write` never runs → the stage still exits 0 (false PASS). Proven on BOTH this row's own pdf/go run (205× `BACKLINK_DOWNGRADE ... refusing write` in the stage log) AND an independent pdf/python run with no interruption at all (`rf-20260829T103745Z-2de981de3bd7`, 204× identical signature) — confirming the interrupted-resume trigger was incidental, not causal, in both this row's original and first-corrected hypotheses. Real fix is `TC-MT060-01B-APPLY-INTEGRITY` in that plan (decision-after-write ordering, typed `WRITE_REFUSED` disposition instead of silence, non-zero stage exit on unreconciled failures, an apply-exit hash-invariant assertion, and a backlink carry-forward fix in `_build_page`) — tracked under `REFRESH-PIPELINE-HARDENING-001` in BACKLOG.md, not this row. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | related-to:MT059-2026-08-31-PDF-GO-S84-FANOUT,MT033-FANOUT-001,REFRESH-PIPELINE-HARDENING-001 | none | 2026-09-01 |

| ST-101 | SYS | KF | `forbidden_claims_check.py`'s tier-2 (`class_with_capability_language`) check flags real, non-fabricated upstream docstring/ERC-fallback prose as a forbidden-capability claim when the prose describes the CLASS's general role or an unrelated method, not the specific forbidden member -- found live 2026-08-29 blocking 4 fresh 3d/python reference pages (HalfSpace, Exporter, ObjImporter, ThreeMfImporter). Two distinct trigger shapes, both real content (not batch_reference.py's own generated boilerplate, which was already fixed for this exact class of issue on pdf/python the same day -- see the two batch_reference.py forbidden-claims commits): (1) HalfSpace's real upstream docstring ("HalfSpace represents a infinity space which is split by a plane, this can be used with BooleanOperator") is flagged for 3 unrelated forbidden members (`__init__`, `remove_property`, `get_property`) purely because "can be used" + bare "HalfSpace" co-occur -- the sentence describes HalfSpace's relationship to a DIFFERENT class (BooleanOperator), not any of the 3 forbidden methods. (2) Exporter's ERC-index class-doc fallback ("Exporter objects let you verify format support with supports_format() before calling export()...") is flagged for `Exporter.supports_format` because "support"/"export" (capability verbs) co-occur with bare "Exporter" as sentence subject -- closer to on-topic than case 1, but still describes checking/calling the method, not claiming it works (the page's own Methods table already correctly marks it "Not implemented in the FOSS edition"). Root cause: tier 2 exists specifically as a broader fallback net for hand-written prose with no specific member named (per its own docstring/tests, e.g. "This class supports FileSystem for direct file access." SHOULD flag) -- but it fires uniformly regardless of whether OTHER, more-specific forbidden claims already exist for named members, and has no way to distinguish "direct claim about the subject" from "incidental mention of the class name in prose that is actually about something else or about checking-not-doing." Not fixed here -- HARDEN-FC-01/FC-02's precedent shows this file's precision boundary is deliberately, carefully tuned (multiple prior review passes), and a rushed regex change to a safety-critical fabrication-prevention check risks a real regression across every product, not just these 4 pages. These 4 pages are left ungenerated (a pre-existing gap, not a new regression -- they behaved identically before this session's other batch_reference.py fixes). Action needed: (1) investigate whether tier 2 should skip a forbidden claim when a MORE SPECIFIC (fully-qualified, tier-1-shaped) claim already exists for the same member elsewhere in the forbidden_claims list for that class, since tier 1 already handles the "actually mentions the method" case precisely; (2) or investigate scoping tier 2 to require the capability verb's grammatical object be the class itself, not merely co-occurrence on the same line/sentence (a real precision improvement, likely needs more than regex); (3) whichever direction, add regression tests reproducing these exact 2 real-content shapes (not just synthetic examples) before changing anything. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | — | none | 2026-08-29 |

| ST-097 | SYS | OM | `refresh_run.py bind-commit` requires the exact commit passed via `--commit` to carry `Refresh-Run`/`Refresh-Base`/`Refresh-Source-SHA` trailers verbatim (`:3121-3139`) -- there is no pre-check that runs BEFORE the commit is built/pushed, so forgetting to include the trailers (easy to do: they're free-text in a `--message-file`, not templated or validated by `git_plumb_commit.py` itself) is only discovered AFTER the commit is already on `origin/main`, and by then the only fix is amending + force-pushing that exact commit -- a destructive operation needing explicit authorization, for what is otherwise a purely cosmetic governance-completeness gap (`check_evidence_churn.py`'s bypass only needs manifest state in `{VALIDATING, READY_TO_COMMIT, COMMITTED}`, which READY_TO_COMMIT already satisfies). Discovered live 2026-08-28 committing the pdf/net batch of the working-tree cleanup plan (commit `12c78845b1`, run `rf-20260828T070658Z-23c955112d74`): the prior 4 batches (barcode/python, email/python, cells/typescript, words/python) all correctly included the trailers and reached COMMITTED; this one didn't and is stuck at READY_TO_COMMIT by user decision (declined the force-push amend rather than risk rewriting already-pushed history) -- functionally fine, but the run's own manifest never gets a `commit_sha` recorded, leaving a slightly misleading audit trail (git history says done, refresh_runs/ says not). Fix candidate: (a) `git_plumb_commit.py` auto-appends these 3 trailers to the message when `--skills` includes S-84 and an active, owned, READY_TO_COMMIT run exists for the touched family/platform (reading run_id/base/target_source_sha straight from the manifest instead of relying on the operator to hand-type them correctly) -- turns a recall-dependent step into a mechanical one; (b) short of that, at minimum a `bind-commit --dry-run` / `refresh_run.py verify-trailers <run-id> <message-file>` pre-check callable BEFORE `git_plumb_commit.py` runs, so a missing trailer is caught pre-push, not post-push. | queued | P3 | S | 0 | — | none | 2026-08-28 | **update:2026-08-29** — Second, more precise failure mode found committing note/python's batch (commit `9fdf922344`): the trailers were correctly INCLUDED this time, yet `bind-commit` STILL reported `found: []`. Root cause identified precisely via `_commit_trailer_values()` (`:3067-3092`): it only recognizes trailers in the commit message's FINAL blank-line-delimited paragraph. This session's own standard commit-message convention appends `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` as its OWN separate trailing paragraph (blank line before it) -- which is ALSO valid trailer syntax, so `_commit_trailer_values`'s backward scan stops there, treating `Co-Authored-By` as the entire trailer block and never reaching the `Refresh-Run`/`Refresh-Base`/`Refresh-Source-SHA` lines one paragraph earlier. Fix candidate (b) above should specifically validate this exact shape (custom trailers + `Co-Authored-By` combined into ONE contiguous block, no blank line between them) since it's the one every real commit in this repo will hit by construction, given the standard commit-message footer convention.

Full taskcard breakdown: plans/from_backlog/2026-08-12-reference-index-duplicate-row-defect.md. **CLOSED 2026-08-12** — deep reassessment (user-directed) found the initial fix (TC-DUPIDX-01/02/03, commit 053330ae05) only worked for .NET/C++/Rust: `_assign_namespace_slug_suffixes()` read only `canonical_namespace`, populated for 0% of Java and TypeScript scout entries (a scout-layer gap, not a bug in the re-invocation mechanism) — regenerating cells/java/cells/python/cells/typescript/pdf/java/words/python did not fix them, and pdf/java got worse (5→28 distinct collisions) as its knowledge model had grown 527→1027 classes since the last real regen. Root cause: a `class_import`-prefix fallback already existed in `consolidate_classes()` (api_surface.py:1737-1746) but was never ported to the one other call site that needed it. Fixed via TC-DUPIDX-06 (extended `canonical_namespace` extraction to Java + TypeScript at the scout layer, commit 1224f6b53d) + TC-DUPIDX-07 (ported the fallback into `_assign_namespace_slug_suffixes()`, same commit) + TC-DUPIDX-08 (re-scouted cells/typescript locally, knowledge/ is gitignored). | ST-058 | S | PF | S-93 `/repo-patrol` and `repo_patrol.py scan` default to `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which 403s on every aspose-*-foss org — discovery silently stalls for 10+ min instead of failing fast. Second session in a row to hit this (2026-07-28 Phase A, then 2026-08-05). Live-verified 2026-08-05: `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITHUB_TOKEN" https://api.github.com/orgs/aspose-pdf-foss/repos` → **403**; same call with `$GH_TOKEN` (classic `ghp_`) → **200**. Root cause per BACKLOG.md header note: the orgs enforce a >366-day-lifetime policy that rejects the fine-grained PAT held in `GITHUB_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_PAT`. Impact: `repo_patrol.py` line 744 (`token = args.token or os.environ.get("GITHUB_TOKEN")`) plus `.claude/commands/repo-patrol.md` step 1 (documents the bare `scan` command with no `--token`) mean the documented invocation hangs on retries and produces no report — a silent discovery outage, not a visible error. This masked a real find: the 2026-08-05 scan surfaced brand-new `psd/python` (INV-014) only after re-running with `--token $GH_TOKEN`. Fix: make the token resolver prefer `GH_TOKEN` then fall back to `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and fail fast with an explicit "403 — token rejected by org policy, use GH_TOKEN" message on the first 403 rather than retrying; update the S-93 skill doc's precondition (currently says only "GITHUB_TOKEN env var set") and step-1 command accordingly. Same defaulting exists in `update_product_registry.py` line 583. | resolved | P2 | S | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-05 | **resolved:2026-08-05** — root cause was deeper than the token. Investigation found the chain converted ANY upstream failure into an empty success: `org_scanner._paginate` treated every 403 as rate-limiting (live 403 carried `X-RateLimit-Remaining: 4993`, so the retry slept 3251s/org ≈ 21h across 25 orgs), then `scan_orgs`'s blanket `except Exception -> []` erased the error, and `cmd_scan` persisted+archived a `total_repos: 0` report at exit 0 — which also reset the 7-day clock the session-start advisory keys on, silencing its own detector. Fixed: 403 auth-vs-rate-limit classification via `X-RateLimit-Remaining`/`Retry-After` + capped backoff; `resolve_token()` (--token > GH_TOKEN > GITHUB_TOKEN) + `preflight()`; typed per-org outcomes; `scan_health` block in patrol_report.json; exit contract 0/1/2; fail-closed writes (neither patrol_report.json nor the TRACKED data/products.json is overwritten on total failure). Docs corrected across skills/repo-patrol.md, session-start.md (now health-aware, not age-only), update-registry.md, getting-started.md, RUNBOOK.md, .env.example, scripts/pipeline/README.md + 3 provider mirrors. Tests 94 -> 117; the old `test_403_triggers_sleep_and_retry`, which asserted the defect was correct, was replaced by a no-sleep-on-auth-403 regression gate. E2E: documented invocation now 36s/exit 0/25-of-25 orgs/33 repos (re-confirming psd/python INV-014); forced fine-grained PAT now fails in 2s at exit 2 with the last good report byte-identical. Gap report: reports/skill-gaps/2026-08-05-discovery-chain-failure-contract.md |

| ST-053 | SYS | LO | `.git/hooks/post-commit` (`consume pending override tokens`) archives **every** file in `reports/overrides/pending/` on any commit, unconditionally -- it never checks whether the commit that just ran actually touches that token's `paths`. Discovered 2026-08-01 (Mission MT018-2026-08-01-PACKAGE-IDENTITY-COVERAGE, TC-MT018-07): created an override token for 4 files (`scripts/pipeline/audit/package_install_literal.py`, `scripts/pipeline/content_eval/evaluators/install_recipe.py`, and their 2 test files), committed via the isolated-temp-index git-plumbing procedure (bypasses `git commit` and its hooks entirely, per CLAUDE.md ST-021 guidance) -- so this session's own commit could not have triggered the hook. A concurrent session's commit (`5859501daa`, "feat(pipeline): add master_plan_index.py...", touching entirely different files) landed via a real `git commit` in between this session's `override_manager.py create` and its own plumbed commit; that commit's post-commit hook found this session's still-pending token in `reports/overrides/pending/` and consumed it, writing `consumed_by_commit: "5859501daa..."` into the archived token JSON -- a commit that never touched any of the 4 paths the token was authorized for. Confirmed via direct read of `scripts/post-commit-override.sh`-installed `.git/hooks/post-commit`: it globs `reports/overrides/pending/*.json` and calls `override_manager.py consume --token-id ... --commit-sha $(git rev-parse HEAD)` for every file found, with no path-vs-diff comparison at all. Not a new phenomenon -- this exact "token not found in pending/" symptom (already-archived before an explicit `consume` call) was observed at least once earlier this same session (TC-MT018-06 closeout) without the mechanism being root-caused at the time. Governance-relevant impact: the archived token's `consumed_by_commit` field is evidentiary -- it is meant to prove "this specific commit is the one this override authorized" -- and is silently wrong under any concurrent-session commit interleaving, which this repo has documented as a routine occurrence (ST-021 and its several related entries). Not fixed here -- out of scope for TC-MT018-07 (install-command gate correctness), filed rather than silently worked around. Fix candidate: `post-commit` should `git diff --name-only HEAD^ HEAD` and only consume a pending token whose `paths` intersect that diff; tokens with no intersecting commit yet should remain pending (or a separate reconciliation pass should re-attribute/re-open mis-consumed tokens). | queued | P3 | S | 0 | MT018-2026-08-01-PACKAGE-IDENTITY-COVERAGE | related-to:ST-021 | 2026-08-01 |

| ST-050 | SYS | OM | S-73 (manual-edit) SKILL.md documents several commands that are stale or fail as written, discovered independently by ~30 parallel S-73 subagent invocations during the 3D-NET-REFRESH-002 remediation (2026-07-31/08-01) — every agent hit the same issues and had to work around them ad hoc, meaning the documented procedure currently cannot be followed literally by a fresh invoker. Four distinct issues: (1) Step 15's literal command `scripts/pipeline/lib/grade_writer.py --files {target_file}` has no CLI/`__main__` entry point at all (confirmed: library-only, `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'core'` when invoked directly) — the actually-wired mechanism is `python -m scripts.pipeline.content_eval evaluate --files {target_file} --write-grade`, which calls the same `grade_writer.write_grade()` internally. (2) Step 16's `skill_run_manager.py record-step` requires an explicit `--run-id` in practice, not optional as the skill text implies — callers must first run `create --skills S-73 --run-type targeted --plan "..."` to obtain one. (3) `change_guard.py` (Step 8) requires `PYTHONPATH=scripts/pipeline` to run at all, undocumented in SKILL.md's invocation examples. (4) `change_guard.py` can false-positive-DENY on a lone bare identifier in `--intent` text (parsed as an unqualified class lookup) — using the dotted `ClassName.member` form avoids it, but this isn't documented as a gotcha anywhere. Separately (not a doc-staleness issue, a harness-level fact worth documenting somewhere): the Edit/Write PreToolUse hook that gates skill-context (Steps "begin"/"end") resolves session identity via the harness's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, which is shared across ALL sibling subagents spawned from the same parent conversation — `AGENT_SESSION_ID` exported via Bash is invisible to the hook's own subprocess (Bash env doesn't persist across tool calls). This causes real, frequent lock-contention between concurrent S-73 invocations on different files (each `begin` can be clobbered by a sibling's `begin` before the paired `Edit` call runs) — every one of ~30 parallel invocations this session hit it at least once. The documented workaround (re-run `skill_context.py begin --scope {file}` immediately before each Edit, retry on collision) reliably works and caused zero incorrect edits, but is undocumented anywhere in SKILL.md or the skill_context.py docstring's user-facing guidance. None of these four+one issues blocked correct execution (all ~30 invocations completed correctly with proper audit trails), but each one costs real time/tokens per invocation to rediscover independently. Fix: update `.agents/skills/manual-edit/SKILL.md` (and its 3 mirrors) Steps 8, 15, 16 with the corrected commands, and add a documented note for the skill-context race + its workaround. Evidence: ~30 individual S-73 audit-trail JSON files under `reports/manual-edits/` from the 2026-07-31/08-01 3d/net remediation session, most of which independently note one or more of these deviations in their own process notes. | queued | P2 | S | 0 | — | related-to:3D-NET-REFRESH-002 | 2026-08-01 |
| ST-047 | SYS | TF | content_eval has a near-total blind spot for member-level API fabrication and wrong-install-command defects — the exact defect classes S-38 truth-audit exists to catch are largely invisible to content_eval's FAIL-level findings and, even when caught as WARN, get routed to the human queue by remediate.py's own triage table, not the LLM-fix pass. Discovered 2026-07-31 during 3D-NET-REFRESH-002 remediation planning: a fresh S-38 truth-audit on 3d/net found 79 FAIL across 27 files (wrong NuGet install command in ~19 files; fabricated API members ColladaLoadOptions, PolygonModifier.Triangulate, MirrorAxis, PbrMaterial.MetallicTexture, BoundingBox.Minimum/.Maximum, etc.) — but a parallel content_eval run on the same product found ZERO FAIL-level findings overlapping any of these defects (content_eval's own 13 FAILs are all unrelated: api_completeness gaps, one prose_claim_binding miss, format_completeness gaps, one empty blog index body, one wrong author field). Two distinct root causes: (1) install_recipe.py's evaluator only emits an INFO-level "no installation instructions" notice when a page has code but no install block — it does not compare an EXISTING install command's package name against install.md at all, so a wrong package name never surfaces as a finding of any severity. (2) code_block_api.py / api_accuracy.py DO catch some fabricated members (Transform.Scale, FileFormat.STL, one of three AssetInfo.Creator instances) but only at WARN severity under category "CB", and remediate.py's `_TRIAGE_RULES` table (scripts/pipeline/content_eval/remediation/triage.py) has no rule for category "CB" — it falls through to the default fallback, which routes WARN findings with no matching category to `FixType.HUMAN`, not `FixType.LLM`. Net effect: S-40 (batch-remediate)'s automated eval→triage→LLM-fix pipeline cannot act on this entire defect class regardless of severity — every occurrence, caught or not, ends up requiring S-73 (manual-edit). Not specific to 3d/net; any product's install-command or member-fabrication defects would hit the same blind spot. Fix candidates for future investigation: (a) add a real package-name comparison to install_recipe.py's `_verify_install_accuracy` (it already reads install.md; it just isn't comparing an existing command's package token against it), (b) add a CB entry to `_TRIAGE_RULES` routing confirmed fabricated-API findings to `FixType.LLM` rather than the default HUMAN fallback. Evidence: reports/audit/3d-net-truth-2026-07-31.md (S-38, 79 FAIL) vs reports/content_eval/eval-20260731-151152.json (content_eval, 0 overlapping FAIL). 2026-07-31/08-01 (Mission MT015-2026-07-30-PACKAGE-REGISTRY): fix candidate (a) resolved, differently than diagnosed. `install_recipe.py`'s H-16 `_verify_install_accuracy()` already existed and does compare an existing install command's package name against `install.md` (confirmed 3d/net's `knowledge/3d/net/merged/install.md` is a real, populated file, not a stub) -- the actual root cause was a subdomain gate: `evaluate()` returned `[]` before ever calling H-16 for any page outside `docs.aspose.org`, so kb/blog/products pages (where most of the 19 wrong-install-command files in 3D-NET-REFRESH-002 actually lived) were silently unchecked. Fixed: H-16 now runs unconditionally across all 4 subdomains; the separate docs-only "missing install instructions" INFO suggestion is unchanged. New test coverage: `scripts/pipeline/tests/test_install_recipe.py` (8 tests -- no test file existed for this evaluator before). Fix candidate (b) (CB-category triage routing in `remediate.py`'s `_TRIAGE_RULES`) is unrelated to package-manager identity and remains open for whoever picks it up next -- not attempted here. | partially_resolved | P2 | M | 0 | MT015-2026-07-30-PACKAGE-REGISTRY | related-to:3D-NET-REFRESH-002 | 2026-08-01 |

| ST-021 | G | OM | Cross-session commit contamination -- BIDIRECTIONAL, observed twice within minutes. (1) Commit e4f02f844f5cdcb3f61b5da4731b3acf18ded006, authored by a DIFFERENT concurrent Claude session working on an unrelated task (email/python format-completeness evaluator fix, TC-RO-002), silently included all 8 files from MY pdf/go docs-group commit-in-progress. (2) Minutes later, the REVERSE happened: I ran `git add content/kb.aspose.org/en/pdf/` and confirmed via `git diff --cached --name-only` that exactly my 5 expected kb files were staged -- but by the time `git commit` actually executed (after ~90s of pre-commit hook checks including a 100-200s go-vet step), the OTHER session had staged ITS OWN 2 barcode/python files into the shared index, and the commit that landed (3f228c8a825be2e020ca00c00e08fb91e543a577) contains ONLY those 2 barcode files -- none of my 5 kb files -- under MY commit message ("content(pdf/go): refresh KB pages for HTML export feature release", full pdf/go body text, Skills invoked: [S-84, S-12, ...S-58...]). My kb files were NOT lost (confirmed still dirty/uncommitted immediately after), so no data loss, but the commit history now has an entry whose message describes pdf/go KB work while its actual diff is barcode/python evaluator-description-wording changes. Root cause confirmed precisely: the race window is the TIME GAP between `git diff --cached --name-only` verification and `git commit` actually executing -- pre-commit hooks (go vet alone took up to 200s in this session) run for a long time between staging and the actual commit object being created, and ANY `git add`/`git restore --staged` by another process during that window changes what actually gets committed, regardless of what was verified staged beforehand. This is fundamentally different from (and worse than) simple stale-token consumption -- it's a live TOCTOU (time-of-check-to-time-of-use) race on the shared git index with a multi-minute window. Not fixed -- nothing pushed yet (git status shows local main only "behind 4", never "ahead"), so purely local and recoverable; re-committing affected work is straightforward, but the false-provenance commits already in local history were left as-is (no git surgery attempted). Prior evidence this session: check_hugo_build.sh override token consumed by an unrelated commit, an index.lock collision, a content_eval override token consumed similarly -- ALL are symptoms of the same root structural gap: this repo's git index is a shared, unscoped, unlocked resource across concurrent agent sessions with no coordination mechanism. Action needed: (1) for immediate safety, re-verify `git diff --cached --name-only` a SECOND time in the same breath as the `git commit` call (no tool calls in between) to shrink the race window as much as possible -- still not airtight given hook runtime, but better than a check-then-wait-then-commit pattern; (2) structurally, this repo needs either single-operator-at-a-time enforcement, per-session git worktrees, or a genuine staging-lock/lease mechanism before concurrent-agent operation can be considered safe for this repo's commit workflow. | queued | P1 | M | 0 | — | related-to:ST-025,ST-027,ST-076 | ~2026-07-14 (original date not recorded — this row predates the Standalone Tasks table's closing-column convention and was never migrated) | **update:2026-08-26** — Third live incident of this exact class, different specific vector: during a large commit-prep session, `git add` (not `git commit`) swept 26 files from a concurrent session's own mid-revert-in-progress into this session's `git diff --staged` output, caught before committing by manual inspection, not by any guard. Root-caused precisely: `scripts/ci/hooks/check_commit_bash_hook.sh` (the hook this repo added specifically to require an active S-76 context before `git commit`) pattern-matches only the literal word `commit` — raw `git add` against the real shared `.git/index` is completely unguarded, confirmed via direct code read (`:151`). This is a specific, actionable gap this row's existing "Action needed" text doesn't name explicitly: extending the same hook pattern to intercept `git add` (comparing staged-diff before/after against the invoking session's own adopted-files list) is a concrete, scoped fix, not a re-statement of the general lock/worktree recommendation already here. Separately (related-to:ST-025, but a distinct specific finding): the sanctioned isolated-index tool `git_plumb_commit.py` — added 2026-08-20 (`reports/skill-gaps/2026-08-20-git-plumb-commit-tier1-mutation-safety.md`) specifically to harden this same commit path — has no divergence/merge-commit handling at all (`commit-tree ... -p head_sha`, single parent only, `:946-948`; straight non-reconciling push, `:964`). Hit twice live this session: a push against a `HEAD` already stale relative to `origin/main` (routine under real concurrent-session load, not an edge case) required hand-building two-parent merge commits via raw plumbing each time, since the tool offers no automated path.
