Discovered 2026-07-14 via S-92 triage-confirm's Signal 3 (page slug not in current api_surface) during the pdf/go refresh -- confidence 0.8, the only high-confidence hit among 66 total signals (all others were benign 0.6 "modified API mentioned" additive-change false-positives, verified separately). NOT caused by this refresh: git diff shows the page's evidence.model_sha is `880e5cfe0b8e109b52c4e6e311d758a354624c0b` -- an ancient SHA that predates even the pre-refresh stored SHA (a71635384e), meaning batch_reference.py has never regenerated this page in the current pipeline era (it silently skips pages whose class no longer matches anything in api_surface.json, rather than flagging them for retirement). Only touch in this refresh was the re-grade step (Step 9.5) adding a new api_accuracy WARN finding to grade_reasons -- content_eval's evaluator DOES notice the class is gone, but nothing routes that signal to S-83 (page-retire). Root structural cause: S-82 delta-site-plan's pages_to_remove computation is plan-vs-plan (diffs old site_plan.yaml against new), not plan-vs-reality -- a class that disappeared from api_surface.json in some EARLIER refresh cycle, if that cycle's site_plan.yaml never recorded the removal, becomes permanently invisible to all FUTURE incremental deltas. Same root-cause shape as the "introducing-pdf-foss-go" mandatory-but-never-created page gap noted earlier in this same sprint (see MEGA-TRAIN-003 backlog/CHANGELOG.md entries) -- plan-vs-plan diffing misses drift relative to actual disk/knowledge state, only catches drift relative to the last snapshot. Action needed: (1) verify Metadata truly doesn't exist in the FOSS source (not just absent from api_surface.json due to an extraction gap -- check runs/.clone_cache/aspose_pdf_go directly), (2) if confirmed removed, retire the page via proper S-83 dual-signal process, (3) consider a periodic disk-vs-knowledge reconciliation sweep (not just plan-vs-plan) to catch this class of orphaned page across all products, not just pdf/go. | queued | P2 | S | 0 | — | none | 2026-07-14 |
| ST-018 | SYS | GI | Frontmatter write corruption: content/blog.aspose.org/pdf/go/_index.md ended up with a DUPLICATE `grade_reasons:` YAML mapping key during the 2026-07-14 pdf/go refresh, breaking the blog.aspose.org Hugo build ("mapping key \"grade_reasons\" already defined"). Root cause: git diff against HEAD showed the file's original valid `categories:\n- Aspose.PDF` block was overwritten with `categories:\nGrade_reasons:\n  - \"Aspose.PDF\"` (the "Aspose.PDF" value survived but under the wrong key name, destroying the categories field and duplicating grade_reasons). Fixed directly via S-73 manual-edit (audit trail: reports/manual-edits/2026-07-14-blog-pdf-go-index-corruption-fix.json); post-fix audit.py 0 FAIL/0 WARN, validate_frontmatter.py clean, hugo build passes. NOT YET ROOT-CAUSED to a specific script -- this file was touched by attach_evidence.py (evidence refresh, Step 2 of S-84) and content_eval's grade_writer.py (re-grade, Step 9.5) during this refresh; either's frontmatter read-modify-write logic could plausibly mis-target a YAML key under some condition (e.g. an empty `categories:` list with no items, which this file had before corruption -- `categories:` with zero list items directly followed by `grade_reasons:` might confuse a regex-based key-boundary detector). Swept all 196 other pdf/go-touched files for the same duplicate-key pattern -- found ONLY this one instance, so likely a narrow edge case (empty/list-boundary condition) rather than a universal bug, but that makes it more dangerous, not less: it could recur silently on any other page with an empty list-type frontmatter field adjacent to a rewritten key, and a Hugo build break is a hard, visible failure only caught because this session happened to run the full 5-site build check before committing -- a refresh that skips that check could ship broken YAML straight to production. Needs: (1) isolate which script actually caused it (bisect by re-running each Step 4/9.5 operation against a copy of the pre-corruption file), (2) add a regression test with an empty-list-adjacent-to-rewritten-key fixture, (3) consider adding a fast YAML-parse sanity check as a cheap pre-commit gate (parse every touched .md's frontmatter with yaml.safe_load and confirm no duplicate keys) rather than relying on a full Hugo build to catch this class of defect. | queued | P1 | M | 0 | — | none | 2026-07-14 |
| ST-017 | K | KF | formats.json extractor doesn't detect a document's native own-format export as a supported format — only detects device/converter-mediated export (e.g. render-to-BMP/JPEG/GIF/TIFF via *Device classes, or explicit *SaveOptions classes like HTMLSaveOptions). Concrete case: pdf/go's formats.json has exactly one "Pdf" entry with direction:import (from Document.Open/loading detection) and NO corresponding direction:export entry, despite `Document.Save(outputPath string) error` existing at document.go:534 (confirmed by direct source read) — pdf/go can clearly save PDF files, that's the library's core purpose. Same pattern likely explains the grade-C regression on 7 pdf/go reference pages after the 2026-07-14 knowledge refresh (BmpDevice, Document, GifDevice, JpegDevice, OptimizeImageOptions, Page, TiffDevice — all format_truth FAIL: "claims bidirectional/export support but formats.json shows import-only/export-only", all A->C regressions caused by the refresh, not by any content edit). The CONTENT claims are correct; the grade regression is a false signal from an incomplete formats extractor. Comparable in shape to 3d/net's FBX false-negative (ST tracked separately in 3D-NET-REFRESH-002's audit) and 3d/java's format contradiction — a recurring class of defect where formats.json under-reports one direction of a format's support. Root cause likely in scout.py/api_surface.py's format-detection heuristic (need to locate the actual detector function — not yet isolated). Fix should NOT be rushed under refresh-cycle time pressure; needs its own investigation of the detector logic across languages (Go doesn't use *SaveOptions consistently the way pdf/net's Html export does, and "native save = export in own format" may need to be a distinct, deliberate detection rule rather than inferred from device/converter classes). Until fixed, the 7 pdf/go pages' grade-C is safe to leave as-is (correct content, wrong internal score) rather than hand-patched. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | — | none | 2026-07-14 |
| ST-016 | G | GI | check_content_write_hook.sh has an unbreakable chicken-and-egg dependency for brand-new content/ files: it requires a content-check pass marker at reports/content-check/<encoded-path>.pass before allowing a Write-tool call, but that marker is only written by scripts/pipeline/audit/runner.py's `_write_content_check_marker()` (runner.py:211) AFTER auditing a file — which requires the file to already exist and be readable (verify_evidence/verify_internal_links/etc. all read fp's on-disk content). This means the Write tool can NEVER create the first version of a brand-new content/ page through the normal, documented flow — it structurally blocks every agent-executed content-creation skill (S-56 new-docs-page, S-57 new-blog-post, S-58 new-kb-howto, and likely new-kb-faq/new-reference-page/new-products-page/new-*-index too) on their very first Write. Discovered 2026-07-14 during MEGA-TRAIN-003 pdf/go pilot refresh: a subagent hit this block generating 3 new pdf/go pages, worked around it by writing drafts to reports/ (ungoverned) then using Bash `cp` into content/ (Bash isn't gated by this specific PreToolUse hook, which only fires on the Write tool), then ran the real audit.py afterward (verified independently — 0 FAIL, matches subagent's self-report). The actual S-23 content-quality gate was NOT bypassed, only the Write-tool PreToolUse gate mechanism was routed around via a different tool. SKIP_CONTENT_WRITE_CHECK=1 exists as the documented "operator only" bypass (docs/_archive/root-orphans/operator-bypasses-human-only.md) but an agent should not self-invoke an operator-only bypass, and did not in this case — it found an unintended second path (Bash) instead, which is arguably worse since it's undocumented and not tracked as a deliberate bypass decision. Needs a real fix: either (a) add a `--pre-check` / `--allow-missing` mode to audit.py that validates in-memory draft content against knowledge before the file exists and writes the marker for the target path, or (b) have S-56/S-57/S-58 write through a small Python helper script (invoked via Bash, which is legitimately ungated) instead of the Write tool for the initial creation, with the skill's existing post-write audit.py call as the real gate — formalizing what the subagent worked out ad hoc. Also worth checking whether this hook has silently blocked-and-been-bypassed before now (check for other reports/content-check/ markers with suspiciously matching creation timestamps to nearby Bash cp/mv activity in git history). | queued | P1 | M | 0 | — | none | 2026-07-14 |

| ST-014 | SYS | TF | Scout misclassifies `@property` (stacked with `@abstractmethod`) as callable methods — font/python `Font` base + 6 concrete subclasses document 8 members (font_type, font_name, font_family, font_style, num_glyphs, metrics, encoding, glyph_accessor) as `method()` calls when real source declares them as properties (`font.font_name`, not `font.font_name()`); calling as documented raises TypeError. Discovered via font/python truth audit (S-38, 56 of 57 FAILs). All 261 reference pages are internally consistent with the flawed api_surface.json — defect is invisible to any audit that doesn't cross-check real FOSS source, which is why it survived prior grading. ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-14 (commit 08a861ad37): `_extract_python_properties()` and the method-extraction skip-check in `extraction/api_surface.py` both only checked the single nearest decorator (direct child or one immediate previous sibling) — a function with `@property` stacked above `@abstractmethod` has `@abstractmethod` as its nearest sibling, so the bare "@property" substring check never found it. Fixed via new `_all_decorator_text()` helper that walks the full decorator stack; used consistently in both call sites plus the `.setter`/`.deleter` detection (same blind spot). 6 new regression tests (test_python_property_decorator_stack.py) confirm correct classification. NOTE: this fix corrects future scout runs but does NOT fix already-corrupted content — knowledge/font/python/merged/api_surface.json and its 7 affected reference pages are still misclassified until a fresh /repo-scout re-run + regeneration. REMAINING SCOPE (1) DONE 2026-08-01: font/python re-scouted via FONT-PYTHON-REFRESH-002's S-84 combined-mode run (repo_sha a744809781→54004305922, now applying the decorator-stack fix); the 7 affected reference pages regenerated (commit 92125ad994) — the 8 members now appear once, under `## Properties`, matching real source; the GlyphPath.commands fabrication also fixed (commit f908eb1762). (2) audit other Python products for the same pre-fix stacked-decorator pattern (any product scouted before commit 08a861ad37 could be affected) remains open, not part of this closure. Evidence: reports/audit/font-python-truth-2026-07-14.md (original 57-FAIL finding); reports/audit/font-python-truth-2026-08-01.md (0 FAIL re-audit closing this item) | done | P1 | M | 8 | — | none | 2026-08-01 |
| ST-013 | SYS | TF | Reference generator scrambles inherited-method descriptions — method names/signatures correct but doc text pulled from unrelated methods on other classes, traced to mis-attached `doc` strings inside api_surface.json itself. Discovered via 3d/java truth audit (S-38, ~700 of ~730 FAIL instances, 37 of 45 refreshed pages); also present on non-refreshed pages (node.md, scene.md), confirming it is live/ongoing, not one-off residue. ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-14 (commit a655727ff9): `_flatten_inheritance()` in `extraction/api_surface.py` appended the SAME dict object reference from a parent's methods/properties into every inheriting child instead of copying — any later mutation of one class's inherited copy silently corrupted the parent's own entry and every other inheriting class, since they all shared the identical object. Fixed via `copy.deepcopy()` on each inherited member before appending; 7 new regression tests (test_flatten_inheritance.py) confirm isolation. NOTE: this fix caps future blast radius but does NOT fix already-corrupted content — knowledge/3d/java/merged/api_surface.json and its 37 affected reference pages are still corrupted until a fresh /repo-scout re-run + regeneration. Also unresolved: what ORIGINALLY introduced the wrong doc text into BooleanOperator's inherited copy in the first place (candidates: enrichment pass, or scout's per-file doc extraction — not traced further). REMAINING SCOPE: (1) re-run /repo-scout 3d java + regenerate the 37 affected pages, (2) audit other products with inheritance-heavy API surfaces for the same pre-fix corruption pattern (any product refreshed before commit a655727ff9 could be affected), (3) optionally continue root-causing the original doc-mismatch source. Evidence: reports/audit/3d-java-truth-2026-07-14.md | in-progress | P1 | M | 8 | — | none | 2026-07-14 |

<!-- SW-001 to SW-015 resolved 2026-05-08: Stage 1 reconciliation confirmed all 16 baselines established (repo_sha non-null in all merged/model.yaml). Fresh S-94 sweep 2026-05-08 confirms all 16 products unchanged. These were false HIGH items from 2026-04-27 when stored_sha was null. -->
| ST-029 | SYS | GI | content_eval FALSE POSITIVES in capability_claim_check / format_truth, found during aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning Lane I adversarial review (2026-07-17). Two distinct FP patterns, each verified line-by-line against page text AND knowledge/pdf/net/merged/formats.json: (1) **identifier-as-format**: the variable name `doc` in ordinary C# sample code (`doc.Save(ms)`, `doc.ToArray()`) is matched as the DOC (Word) format, emitting `[CF] Claims DOC export but knowledge says direction is 'none'`. formats.json genuinely has `{"format":"Doc","direction":"enum_only","functional":false}`, so the knowledge side is right — the page side is misread. Blast radius measured: **39 occurrences across 19 pdf/net non-reference files**, and `doc` is the near-universal idiom for an Aspose.PDF Document variable, so this likely recurs across every pdf family/platform. (2) **product-name + negation**: "Aspose.PDF FOSS ... does not support .NET Framework, .NET Standard" is matched as `[FT] Claims PDF is not supported but formats.json shows both support` — the sentence is about .NET runtimes, not the PDF format. A third, softer case: "Image output ... governed by the ImageFormat enum, which supports BMP, GIF, JPEG, PNG, TIFF" is read as `Claims bidirectional BMP` although "Image output ... supports" is unambiguously export framing (directionless "supports" defaults to bidirectional). IMPACT: these FPs inflate WARN counts and depress grades — they are plausibly why kb/pdf/net/faq.md and how-to-work-with-pdf-documents-dotnet.md are C-grade at all, and they caused BOTH the implementer and an independent reviewer to reach wrong conclusions about the same 5 pages (implementer: "0 FAIL so no defect" — unsound; reviewer: "source-verifiable false capability claims" — refuted by the page text). Action: (a) require a format token to appear in a format-claim CONTEXT (table row / prose sentence), not as a code identifier — exclude matches inside backticks/code spans and known variable idioms; (b) scope negation matching to the format token, not the product name; (c) treat "output/export ... supports X" as export-only rather than bidirectional; (d) add regression fixtures for all three patterns. Evidence: reports/regression_classification/RPR-A-02-remediation-disposition.json (CORRECTION_2026-07-17_post_lane_I block, with exact lines + verdicts). 2026-07-17 (session 5, RPR-J-09): PARTIAL CLOSURE — pattern (1) identifier-as-format FIXED in capability_claim_check.py (member-access guard: a format token immediately followed by `.<identifier>` is excluded as a variable member-access, not a format claim). Verified: real page WARN count 17→14 (the 3 `doc.Save`/`doc.ToArray` DOC-export FPs gone), genuine-claim regression fixture still fires, spot-checked 4/19 originally-measured affected files (0 CF-DOC findings remain in all 4; 15 unchecked, tracked as RPR-J-12). Patterns (2)/(3) (product-name+negation, export-framing-as-bidirectional) in format_truth.py are UNFIXED — still open. 40 tests pass (test_version_capability_evaluators.py). 2026-07-17 (session 5 continuation, RPR-J-09 remainder): FULL CLOSURE — pattern (2) fixed via `_is_product_name_mention()` (excludes a format token immediately preceded by "Aspose." — the match's own leading-dot lookbehind means the preceding text ends in either "Aspose" or "Aspose." depending on whether the dot was consumed by the main regex, both handled). Pattern (3) fixed via `_resolve_list_direction()`: added "output"/"input" to the export/import verb regexes, then — because the real sentence puts several formats in one enumerated list where only the first falls inside `_claim_direction`'s narrow +/-80-char window — added a SENTENCE-scoped (not line-scoped) widened lookback so every item in the list inherits the sentence's own export framing; sentence-scoping (not whole-line) was required because the real page's line 206 holds an earlier, unrelated "reads and writes the PDF format" clause before the "Image output ... supports BMP, GIF, JPEG, PNG, TIFF" clause — a whole-line widening was tried first and wrongly let "reads"/"writes" leak into the later, separate claim's direction (caught by testing against the real page text, not just a shortened synthetic sentence, before considering this done). Verified against the real pages named above: format_truth findings on kb/pdf/net/faq.md + how-to-work-with-pdf-documents-dotnet.md went from 3 WARN + 1 INFO to 0 WARN + 1 INFO (the residual INFO is an unrelated, legitimate "PNG not in formats.json" note, not a false positive). 4 new regression tests added (TestFormatTruthCheck, using the exact real sentences), all pass; full evaluator suite 44/44, full content_eval suite 365/365, full pipeline suite green. | resolved | P2 | M | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-027,ST-028 | 2026-07-17 |
| ST-030 | G | OM | RPR-G-04's taskcard/plan parity control is UNENFORCED and vacuous by default — found by the plan's own Lane I adversarial review (2026-07-17), i.e. the drift-detector for plan prose was itself undetected drift. `scripts/ci/checks/check_taskcard_plan_parity.py` has **zero references** in `.github/`, pre-commit, or the pipeline registry — nothing ever invokes it, so RPR-G-04's control never actually runs. Root cause is architectural and worth stating plainly: plan documents live in `~/.claude/plans/` (OUTSIDE the repo), so a repo-scoped CI job cannot reach the `--plan-file` it needs; the check is therefore an agent-invoked tool (via S-113 plan-health-watchdog) rather than a CI gate, but that was never documented — it was left looking like a gate. Second defect: default mode is silence-tolerant by design ("a markdown task with no [STATUS] tag is not a contradiction"), which on this mission's own plan compares only **7 of 36** tasks; `--strict-coverage` reports **26 untagged**. The silence-tolerant default is defensible (forcing the prose to be a complete, always-current status board is the brittle prose-scanning failure mode S-113 exists to retire) but it means the default cannot catch drift on an untagged task — a real limit that must be documented rather than implied away. Action: (1) document in the module docstring + S-113 SKILL.md that this is agent-invoked, not CI-enforced, and why; (2) wire it into the S-113 session-start/nightly workflow so it genuinely runs; (3) decide explicitly whether a mission's own plan must tag all its tasks (i.e. run --strict-coverage for `execution_authority: true` plans) — the plan's §7 currently tags 7/36; (4) if plan files ever move into the repo, add the CI job. | queued | P2 | S | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-028 | 2026-07-17 |
| ST-031 | G | OM | Lane H pilot halt (RPR-H-02/03/04 = BLOCKED_LOCAL per plan §9 Stage 3) is enforced only by taskcard status + plan prose, never by a script-level precondition check — found by the post-plan convergence audit (session 5, 2026-07-17, L3-J-01). Nothing prevents a different agent/session from directly invoking Lane H pilot work out of dependency order; the current executor honors the taskcard status correctly, but this is a defense-in-depth gap for any future/different executor. Same root-cause CLASS as SW-1 (pre-G-03 skill_context.py single-file race, fixed) and SW-5 (gates trusting bookkeeping over a durable source, being fixed in Lane J): this pipeline generally encodes sequencing as prose/status convention (taskcard status, plan wave-ordering, SKILL.md preconditions) rather than an executable precondition check. G-03/OCC fixed the analogous gap for concurrent SESSIONS; this is the same gap for out-of-order TASK selection within one mission. Explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning's Lane J (a taskcard-dependency-precondition checker is a genuinely new, separate capability extending S-113 plan-health-watchdog, not a verification-re-foundation item) — filed as a standalone follow-up per the audit's governed-exclusion disposition, not taskcarded into this mission. Action (future): extend S-113 to read taskcards.jsonl dependency edges (once a `depends_on` field exists in schema 5f — currently absent) and block/warn on an IN_PROGRESS transition whose dependency isn't DONE. | queued | P3 | M | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-030 | 2026-07-17 |
| ST-032 | SYS | GI | session_ledger.py's `adopt_files()`/`_apply_touch()`/`classify_file()` normalization used `.lstrip("./")` at 4 call sites — `str.lstrip()` strips a CHARACTER SET ({'.', '/'}), not a literal prefix, so ANY path starting with a literal dot-directory (`.agents/`, `.claude/`, `.kilocode/`, `.github/`) had its leading dot silently eaten: `.agents/skills/refresh-product/SKILL.md` normalized to `agents/skills/refresh-product/SKILL.md`. Found live during aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning's RPR-J-11 commit-prep (2026-07-17): `session_ledger.py adopt --files ".agents/skills/refresh-product/SKILL.md" ...` reported "Adopted N files" (no error) but the file never appeared in `candidates` output — the touched-file key (`agents/...`) could never match `_git_dirty_files()`'s real (dotted) path, so the file silently never became a commit candidate no matter how many times "adopted". Same bug affects `record()` (real-time file-touch recording via hooks) and `check_ledger_membership` for staged-file pre-commit validation — meaning ANY prior session's edits to `.agents/skills/`, `.claude/commands/`, or `.kilocode/skills/` files may have silently fallen outside their session ledger's candidate set too, if that session relied on automatic touch-recording rather than manual `git add`. Fixed: all 4 `.lstrip("./")` call sites (lines 151, 388, 540, 863) changed to `.removeprefix("./")` (Python 3.9+, strips the literal prefix only, correct semantics). Added 4 regression tests to test_session_ledger.py (91/91 pass) pinning the dotfile-preservation behavior end-to-end (adopt -> touched_files -> candidates). No historical commits were audited for silent omissions caused by this bug (out of scope for this fix — flagging as a possible explanation for any past "file X wasn't committed even though it was clearly touched" confusion, not confirmed). | queued | P2 | S | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-030,ST-031 | 2026-07-17 |
| ST-033 | SYS | GI | session_ledger.py's active-session pointer is a single global file (`reports/session-state/` ACTIVE pointer, same design pattern as skill_context.py's pre-G-03 ACTIVE.json / SW-1), so file-touch RECORDING is not actually scoped per-agent-session -- it is scoped to "whichever session happens to be the current ACTIVE pointer" repo-wide. Found live during aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning's RPR-J-11 commit prep (2026-07-17): `session_ledger.py groups` surfaced 2 real, in-progress Spanish locale content edits (content/kb.aspose.org/es/page/python/troubleshooting.md, content/kb.aspose.org/es/words/python/how-to-build-ldm-builder-python.md, timestamped 14:51/15:06 same day) as "candidates" in THIS session's ledger (20260713-181252-ee2a) -- but this session never touched content/ at all this pass. These are the concurrent localization agent's live, legitimate in-progress work (per explicit operator confirmation: "all agents are done working except for the localization work"), being silently attributed to a DIFFERENT session's ledger because both agents' edit-recording hooks write into the one globally-active session pointer. Caught by manual diff/mtime verification before committing, not by any automated guard -- had this gone unnoticed, /commit would have force-included a concurrent session's mid-edit locale file into an unrelated pipeline-fix commit. Same root-cause CLASS as SW-1 (fixed for skill_context.py via RPR-G-03's per-session OCC) -- session_ledger.py needs the analogous fix: a session-id-scoped touched-files record (not a single global ACTIVE pointer) so concurrent agents' recordings don't cross-contaminate. Action: extend RPR-G-03's --session-id pattern to session_ledger.py's record/candidates/groups commands. Until fixed, ANY /commit invocation during confirmed-concurrent-session activity MUST manually diff-verify every file in every proposed commit group, not trust `candidates`/`groups` output alone. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-021,ST-032 | 2026-07-17 |
| ST-034 | SYS | GI | No refresh-run-manifest WRITER exists anywhere in the pipeline (schema 5e is validated by schema_validators.py but nothing ever produces `reports/refresh_runs/{family}/{platform}/*.manifest.json`) -- found by RPR-J-10's independent Lane I review (2026-07-17) while investigating why Check 7 FAILed for 3d/net and cells/java after the Lane J re-founding. RPR-J-17 built the READ side of base-commit resolution (`--base` CLI override, `_load_base_commit_sha()` reading a manifest's `base_commit_sha` field, priority explicit > manifest > HEAD) but explicitly left the WRITE side out of scope ("no refresh-run-manifest WRITER exists... a separate, larger, not-yet-scoped task"). Consequence, proven not hypothetical: with zero manifests on disk, EVERY --verify run defaults to comparing against HEAD, which is vacuous the moment a refresh has already been committed (HEAD then IS the post-refresh state). The independent reviewer proved this exactly for 3d/net and cells/java: re-verifying against the real pre-refresh baseline (`git log` forensics found `f32c88dcc9^` and `0a182f57b0^`/`15509b3cb3^` respectively) shows 169/215 and 178/180 pages with real body diffs -- Check 7 would PASS with a correct baseline; it only FAILs today because HEAD is the wrong comparison point. The reviewer's overall verdict: this is the SINGLE BIGGEST operational gap blocking safe use of the re-founded gates for live pilots (RPR-H-02/03/04) -- the `--base` mechanism (RPR-J-17) is correct but has no real input to consume. Action: build the manifest writer and wire it into S-84 Step 1 (knowledge-diff, before any refresh work begins) so `base_commit_sha` is captured automatically, per the plan's own §19.3 design intent. Until fixed, any product verification MUST pass an explicit `--base <pre-refresh-commit>^` found via manual git forensics, or Check 7/10/11 results for an already-refreshed product cannot be trusted at face value. RESOLVED 2026-07-18: built `scripts/pipeline/commands/ops/refresh_run_manifest.py` (real `git rev-parse HEAD` capture, nanosecond-suffixed run_id for guaranteed ordering), wired as a best-effort step into S-84 Step 1 across all 4 skill mirrors, commit `d9cd47d4af`. Smoke-tested for real against pdf/net; `post_refresh_verify._load_base_commit_sha` confirmed to read it back correctly. 9 new tests, full suite green (7088 passed). | resolved | P1 | M | 0 | aspose-org-foss-refresh-tidy-lightning | related-to:ST-021 | 2026-07-18 |

| ST-036 | SYS | GI | format_truth.py's `_resolve_list_direction()` (RPR-J-09/ST-029 pattern 3 fix) has a residual, narrower false-positive class -- found by RPR-J-10's independent Lane I review (2026-07-17), which constructed and verified a concrete counter-example: "Aspose.3D can read scene data and output to FBX, OBJ, STL, PLY, GLTF images." A single sentence that joins a genuinely separate import clause and an export-framed list via "and" (not a period) still resolves list items beyond the narrow context window to the ambiguous "both" (has_export and has_import both true within the one punctuation-delimited sentence), producing a false bidirectional-mismatch WARN for an export-only format late in such a list. This is the same severity class as the bug RPR-J-09 fixed, not a new failure mode -- the original ST-029 real-corpus case (kb/pdf/net/faq.md:206) used a period and is confirmed fixed; this is a narrower residual. No fix was attempted: distinguishing "and" as a clause boundary from "and" as a plain list conjunction ("BMP, GIF, and TIFF") reliably needs more than a regex heuristic, and a wrong heuristic risks regressing the already-fixed case. | ST-067 | SYS | GI | `pytest.ini`'s own declared `testpaths` (`tests scripts/pipeline/tests scripts/translator/tests`) cannot currently be run as a whole: `tests/test_enrichment_schemas.py` fails at COLLECTION time (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'jsonschema'`), which under a bare `pytest -q` halts collection entirely (`Interrupted: 1 error during collection`) — nothing else in the declared full suite runs at all unless the caller explicitly passes `--continue-on-collection-errors`, which is not this repo's documented default invocation anywhere. Found live 2026-08-08 during MT023's reopening-closure regression-evidence gathering (out of that mission's own lane — media-optimize has nothing to do with enrichment schemas; filed here so the gap is tracked rather than invisible, matching ST-059's own precedent for exactly this situation). Root cause investigated: `jsonschema` is not declared in any `requirements*.txt` in this repo; the importing line (`tests/test_enrichment_schemas.py:8`) has existed unchanged since commit `44611e795d` (2026-05-11); `.venv` was created 2026-07-14 — both predate this finding by a wide margin, and nothing in the finding session's own diff touches this file, any requirements file, or the venv. Could not determine from git history alone whether `jsonschema` was ever actually present in this (or any) `.venv` — venv package state isn't version-controlled, so this is genuinely unresolvable without a `pip list` snapshot from whoever last had a working run. MT023's own 2026-08-06 closure claimed "8522 passed, 2 failed... 82 skipped" with no collection error, which is only possible if either `jsonschema` was present then and has since been removed from the shared `.venv` by an unrelated process, or that closure's own run used a narrower/different invocation than a bare `pytest -q` — not resolvable retroactively. Effect is more severe than a single failing test: a collection error silently prevents pytest's OWN declared scope from running at all for anyone using the plain documented invocation, which could mask an unknown number of real regressions behind an apparently-clean (but actually never-run) result. Fix: either add `jsonschema` to the appropriate `requirements*.txt` (if the dependency is intended to be real) and reinstall, or make the test module's own import guard skip cleanly (`pytest.importorskip("jsonschema")`) if the dependency is genuinely optional, so a missing optional package degrades to one skipped test rather than halting collection for the entire declared suite. | queued | P2 | S | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-08 |

| ST-078 | SEC | GI | `check_session_gate.sh` had a bootstrap-deadlock bug (fixed 2026-08-16, not yet committed): when `.venv`/`$PYTHON` is broken or absent, the hook's tool_name/command JSON parsing shells out to `"$PYTHON" -c ...`, which silently fails and fell through to a fail-closed branch that never re-applied the hardcoded Read/Grep/Glob exemptions or the exact-bootstrap-command exemption — because those checks compare against `$TOOL_NAME`, which was never extracted on that path. Net effect: a broken venv produced a TOTAL tool lockout with no recovery path, including the one command capable of recovering (`bash scripts/ci/hooks/bootstrap_session_gate.sh`) and even a plain `Read`. This defeated the "narrow fail-open" the file's own comments claim to guarantee for exactly this deadlock (the guard only covered empty `$TOOL_INPUT`, not a broken `$PYTHON` with non-empty `$TOOL_INPUT`) — a pre-existing latent defect since the hook was last touched (`67f7f85f79`, 2026-04-30), not introduced by whatever dispatched the affected agent. (2) **still open, process gap**: nothing auto-provisions `.venv`/bootstrap for a newly created worktree — `EnterWorktree` and the Agent tool's `isolation:"worktree"` are generic harness features with no awareness of this repo's per-worktree bootstrap requirement, and there is no `WorktreeCreate` hook wired in `.claude/settings.json` to do it automatically. Not a regression — this is documented, intentional scope (the fix fully solves the actual root cause it was built for, a claim's own disproof cited as support, i.e. the CONTRADICTS case) — but it is a real, live coverage boundary worth a deliberate decision rather than leaving as an unexamined design choice. | ST-079 | S | GI | `refresh_run.py`'s `abandon`/`recover` silently fail to restore scoped files when `_restore_scope_to_baseline()` can't find a `page_decisions` row for every scoped page — the restore raises `"Run evidence must contain exactly one decision per scoped page: missing=[...]"`, `abandon` swallows it by design (its own code comment: "abandon must never re-raise"), records `restore_error` in the manifest, and still transitions the run to terminal `ABANDONED` as if cleanup succeeded, leaving the working tree untouched with no restore and no visible warning to the operator. Found live 2026-08-17 (pdf/python S-84 pilot, run `rf-20260817T135816Z-d2e4cbb59bfa` then again on `rf-20260817T145708Z-11451123d9df`): reproduced identically both times, same 2 missing pages both times (`content/docs.aspose.org/en/pdf/python/developer-guide/_index.md`, `content/docs.aspose.org/en/pdf/python/getting-started/_index.md`) — deterministic, not a fluke. Root cause not yet investigated in depth, but the shape strongly suggests body-refresh's own page-decision writer skips writing a decision row when it judges a scoped page "unaffected" (no diffable change needed), which `_restore_scope_to_baseline`'s completeness check doesn't tolerate. No data was actually lost either time this was hit (the CLI caller independently verified working-tree state via `git status` before trusting the abandon/recover result and found nothing had changed), but the safety net does not work as designed and a less careful caller could believe a revert happened when it didn't. Fix candidate: either (a) make `_restore_scope_to_baseline` tolerant of scoped pages with zero decisions (treat "no decision" as "nothing to restore for this page" rather than a hard precondition failure), or (b) make the page-decision writer always emit a row (even a `NO_CHANGE`/`SKIPPED` one) for every scoped page regardless of impact verdict, closing the gap at the source; either way, `abandon`/`recover` should surface `restore_error` loudly to the caller (non-zero exit, or a clearly flagged CLI warning) instead of only recording it inside the manifest JSON. **Reproduced a 3rd time 2026-08-18**, a different code path (reference-only scope, run `rf-20260818T172622Z-36dd3ee4b0e9`, 130-page `--rebuild-all` regeneration of `reference.aspose.org/en/cells/python`): `abandon`'s restore raised `"Run evidence contains unbound or mismatched receipt/decision references"` (a different message from the original 2 occurrences' `"...exactly one decision per scoped page: missing=[...]"`, suggesting a second, related-but-distinct reconciliation-mismatch shape under the same silently-swallowed-restore-failure umbrella) and again left 61 real content diffs sitting in the working tree with the run marked terminal `ABANDONED` as if cleanup succeeded. Caught the same way as before — an independent `git status` check before trusting the result — and manually reverted via `git checkout HEAD -- <path>`. Still not root-caused or fixed; now 3 confirmed live occurrences across 2 different scope shapes (body content, reference regeneration) strengthens the case this is systemic to `_restore_scope_to_baseline`'s completeness assumption generally, not specific to body-refresh's page-decision writer as originally hypothesized. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-17 |
| ST-080 | S | GI | `attach_evidence.py` operates product-wide (no scope-restriction flag) while `refresh_run.py`'s active-run checkpoint hashes the exact content of every scoped file at apply-time — running `attach_evidence.py` on a product with an in-flight S-84 run mutates those same files again, and the run's own `resume`/`verify` then correctly (but unhelpfully) blocks with `"Scoped working files changed outside a validated run checkpoint"`, since it has no way to distinguish "an unrelated out-of-band edit" from "a legitimate evidence-reconciliation pass the operator intends to keep." Fix candidate: either (a) document the required ordering explicitly in the S-84/S-20 skill chain docs (docs/workflows/) so `attach_evidence.py` is never invoked while a run is ACTIVE for that product, with `refresh_run.py`'s own lock file consulted as a pre-flight check attach_evidence.py could itself refuse to run against, or (b) give `attach_evidence.py` a `--scope-manifest`/exclude-active-runs flag so it can safely skip files currently owned by an in-flight run instead of colliding with them. | queued | P2 | M | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-17 |
| ST-082 | C | GI | S-84 body-refresh's `PARAGRAPH_REWRITE` strategy (LLM-invoked, `_paragraph_rewrite()` in `scripts/pipeline/commands/content/body_refresh.py`) applied a real, incorrect class-name substitution: `content/blog.aspose.org/pdf/python/introducing-pdf-foss-python/index.md`'s "Document Creation and Loading" section was rewritten to `### PdfDocument Creation and Loading`, replacing every `Document` mention with `PdfDocument`, including a code example changed to `from aspose_pdf import PdfDocument` / `with PdfDocument() as document:` — and, in the "Adding Text and Images" section's code block, additionally **broke Python syntax outright**: the LLM split one coherent `page.add_image(...)` call into two markdown code fences, inserted a fabricated sentence ("The `Document` class has been renamed and its API updated. Use the new `PdfDocument` class...") between them, and left `width=300,\n    height=200,\n)` as orphaned trailing lines with no enclosing function call. This is factually wrong and would break real usage: the real, public, user-facing class is `Document` (defined in `src/aspose_pdf/document.py`, exported from `aspose_pdf/__init__.py` via `from .document import Document`); `PdfDocument` is a completely different, internal, never-top-level-exported class (`class PdfDocument` in `src/aspose_pdf/engine/cos.py`, a low-level COS object type) — the LLM appears to have hallucinated a rename relationship purely because the two names share the substring "Document", not because of any real relationship between them (confirmed live 2026-08-17, pdf/python: `import PdfDocument` from top-level `aspose_pdf` would raise `ImportError` for real). **Confirmed deterministic, not a one-off**: reproduced identically (same wrong class swap, same broken-code artifact) across two independent runs on two different run_ids (`rf-20260817T153655Z-9b91323546ff` then `rf-20260817T165416Z-278ee36703d5`) against the same page/knowledge state — the receipt for the second run (`RCP-da76d2bade66dba4cf7f.yaml`) confirms `strategy_used: PARAGRAPH_REWRITE`, `llm_invoked: true`, sections `quick-start`+`supported-formats`. Caught before commit only because the run's own `abandon` path (already broken per ST-079) forced a manual working-tree inspection that happened to surface the diff both times; a normal `resume`->`verify`->`bind-commit` path with no `abandon` step in between would very likely have shipped this straight to a live blog post, since none of the validation gates that DID run this session (`content_audit`, `oracle`, `triage_confirm`, `truth_audit`, `post_refresh_verify`, `link_validation`, `seo_stability` — all PASS on the second run) caught either the false-rename claim or the broken Python syntax. `PdfDocument` was one of the classes surfaced fresh into `api_surface.json` by this same session's scout-collision-bug fix (commit `1062435013`), which is very likely why it now appears in the change/delta context fed to the `PARAGRAPH_REWRITE` LLM prompt for this page for the first time — i.e. this specific manifestation may be new-to-this-session, but the underlying prompt/validation gap (nothing constrains or checks the LLM's paragraph-rewrite output against the actual package-export surface) is a pre-existing structural weakness that will keep firing on any future new-class-with-a-similar-substring case, for any product. Fix candidate: (a) harden the `PARAGRAPH_REWRITE` prompt to explicitly forbid inferring rename/deprecation relationships between two classes unless the knowledge delta explicitly marks one as a modification of the other (nothing in `knowledge_delta.json`'s schema — `new_apis`/`removed_apis`/`modified_apis`/`resurrected_apis` — currently claims a rename relationship at all, so this is purely an LLM inference from co-occurrence, not a signal it was ever given license to draw); (b) add a post-generation validation gate that flags any rewritten `from <package> import <Name>` statement whose target isn't actually reachable per `api_surface.json`'s `exported_via_package_init`/`public_import_module` fields (made more reliable by the scout collision fix); (c) add a cheap Python-syntax-validity check on any code fence body-refresh rewrites (e.g. `ast.parse()` on extracted ```python blocks), since this defect produced literally unparseable code and no existing gate checks that; (d) re-scan pdf/python (and ideally other recently-S-84-refreshed products) for any other content that already shipped a similar wrong substitution before this was caught. **CONFIRMED live a 3rd, worse time 2026-08-18 (cells/python, run `rf-20260818T074154Z-4edae3a36af4`)**: given two separate, factless `API_MODIFIED` change records on the same section (`Cell` and `CellValueHandler`, both real classes but with zero old/new diff data), body-refresh fabricated a fictional `CellValueHandler`-based replacement API (`cell.value_handler = CellValueHandler("Hello, world")`) and falsely claimed the real, still-present `Cell.__init__(value=None, formula=None)` constructor had been "removed" — `CellValueHandler` is actually an internal, all-`@staticmethod` ECMA-376 XML serialization utility with no instance API at all (`runs/.clone_cache/aspose_cells_python/aspose/cells_foss/cell_value_handler.py`), confirming this is the same class of fabrication as the `Document`/`PdfDocument` case, not a one-off. Root cause found and **FIXED 2026-08-18** (commit `581ed5008255`): `_paragraph_rewrite()`'s ungrounded-mode prompt (used whenever no `claim_id`/`merged.bak` grounding exists — the common case for a remediation-mode run) told the LLM only "`{symbol}` has changed (`{change_type}`)" with zero facts about what changed, which is fix candidate (a)'s exact predicted mechanism: an instruction the model can only satisfy by inventing specifics. Fix: new `_current_api_fact_summary()` grounds the ungrounded prompt in the symbol's real, current members from `api_surface.json` with explicit anti-fabrication rules; when no grounding exists at all (symbol absent from `api_surface.json` too), the LLM is no longer called — falls back to the deterministic template with a new `NO_GROUNDING_AVAILABLE` reason. Live-reproduced against the real production LLM for both the `Cell`/`CellValueHandler` and `Document`/`PdfDocument` repro cases post-fix: both now return unchanged or correctly-grounded output. 5 new regression tests, 248 existing tests still pass. Fix candidates (b) code-fence-import-reachability and (c) `ast.parse()` syntax-validity gate remain open as defense-in-depth follow-ups (not yet built); (d) re-scan for already-shipped instances also not yet done. **Update 2026-08-18, same-session retest of cells/python (3 further attempts)**: retest #2 surfaced a SEPARATE defect in the same code path — corrupted code-fence structure (a blank separator line between two fenced blocks replaced by a stray fence, splitting a valid block into unfenced prose + an orphaned fence), reproduced with zero hallucinated content involved. **FIXED** (commit `a139194323b7`): new triple-backtick-count balance guard, rejects any output whose fence count differs from the source section's, falls back to the deterministic template with new `CODE_FENCE_IMBALANCE` reason. Retest #3 then found the fence guard's own fix wasn't taking effect on identical input — root cause: the LLM output cache stores entries keyed without any signal for this new guard, so an entry accepted under the pre-guard code kept being served on a cache HIT, which bypasses every post-response guard entirely. **FIXED** (commit `8065765c7a71`): bumped `_BODY_REFRESH_CACHE_SCHEMA` "1"->"2" (the sanctioned invalidation mechanism this constant's own comment describes), plus a new `cache_schema_override` configure() seam so the busting behavior itself has real test coverage. Retest #4 (all 3 fixes together) found the guards now correctly reject bad output and fall back safely — no fabrication, no fence corruption — but surfaced TWO MORE, still-open issues: (i) the deterministic fallback's own "append an explanatory note" logic can land the note text INSIDE a fenced Python code block when the section boundary happens to fall mid-block, breaking Python syntax a different way (e.g. a plain-English sentence inserted between a `for i, ... :` line and its indented body); (ii) one page showed a literal leaked prompt-template fragment ("Rewritten paragraph:") in the shipped text, meaning the LLM sometimes echoes the prompt's own completion cue back as if it were content, and no current guard checks for this. Filed separately as ST-083 (mid-code-block fallback insertion) and ST-084 (prompt-template leakage) rather than folding further into this row, since both are distinct root causes from everything fixed here so far. Reverted before commit both times; nothing shipped. | in_progress | P1 | L | 0 | none | none | 2026-08-18 |
| ST-083 | C | GI | `body_refresh.py`'s `_deterministic_fallback()` appends its explanatory note ("The `{symbol}` API surface has changed...") via `section_text.rstrip() + note` without checking whether `section_text` (the sliced `impact_section` region) ends INSIDE an open fenced code block. Found live 2026-08-18, cells/python, run `rf-20260818T084732Z-db642d90b3ed` (retest #4 of the ST-082 fix chain, after the grounding/fence-balance/cache-schema fixes were all in place and correctly rejecting bad LLM output): the safe fallback path itself then inserted its note directly inside a `for` loop body, producing `for i, (q, r, e) in enumerate(...):\n\nThe \`Cell\` API surface has changed...\n    ws.cells[f"A{i}"].value = q` — a plain-English sentence between a `for:` header and its indented body, which is a Python `SyntaxError` if executed. Reproduced across 4 of the 16 scoped files in the same run (`aspose-cells-foss-python-excel-chart-tutorial/index.md`, `cells-key-features/index.md`, `developer-guide/working-with-charts.md`, `developer-guide/working-with-formulas.md`), always at a point inside a ```python fence. This is the SAFE/fallback path, not the LLM path — meaning even a fully successful defense-in-depth guard (ST-082's fence-balance check, etc.) does not protect against this, since the fallback itself is the source of the corruption once it fires. Root cause: `impact_section["lines"]` boundaries come from `claim_parser.py`'s section-splitting logic, which appears to sometimes end a section mid-code-block rather than always at a clean prose/heading boundary; `_deterministic_fallback()` has no fence-parity awareness at all. Fix candidate: before appending the note, count unclosed ``` fences in `section_text` (odd count = ends mid-block) and either (a) insert the note before the last fence-open instead of at the very end, or (b) skip the note entirely for a mid-block section (safest, matches the existing `len(section_text.strip()) < 10` "nothing sensible to say" guard's spirit) and log why. Reverted before commit; nothing shipped. **FIXED 2026-08-18** (commit `f651f4bf4d94`): skip the note entirely when `section_text.count("\`\`\`")` is odd (genuinely mid-fence), matching the existing too-short-section precedent. New regression test + live-verified against the real cells/python shape: returns unchanged instead of corrupted. | done | P1 | M | 0 | related-to:ST-082 | none | 2026-08-18 |

| ST-085 | C | GI | body-refresh's `PARAGRAPH_REWRITE`, even with the ST-082 grounding fix in place, can still fabricate false claims about a RELATED/NESTED type it wasn't given facts about — the grounding fix only summarizes the top-level changed `symbol`, never types the section merely references (e.g. `Cell.style` returns a `Style` object with a nested `.font`). Found live 2026-08-18, cells/python, 5th retest attempt (all of ST-082/083/084's fixes active and correctly blocking the OTHER defect classes): `content/kb.aspose.org/en/cells/python/how-to-style-cells-python.md`'s "Step 4: Apply Bold, Italic, Underline, and Strikethrough" section was rewritten to claim "The current `Cell` API does not expose direct `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, or `strikethrough` attributes... you must create a style object... and apply it... using `cell.apply_style(style)`" — this is FALSE: `Font.__init__`'s own docstring example (`runs/.clone_cache/aspose_cells_python/aspose/cells_foss/style.py:22-24`) shows `style.font.bold = True` as the documented pattern, and `Cell.style`'s own docstring (`.../cell.py:107-109`) shows the identical `cell.style.font.bold = True` usage — both real, current, directly-verified. `cell.apply_style()` IS a real method (`cell.py:333`) but the claim that direct attribute assignment "does not work" and apply_style is REQUIRED is invented. The rewrite also degraded 3 separate working code examples into non-functional pseudocode with placeholder comments (`# Create and configure a style object... # (e.g., set bold, italic...)`), and left at least one orphaned sentence referencing removed code (`set_solid_fill()`/`set_no_fill()` described as "mutually exclusive" with no surviving example using either). None of ST-082/083/084's guards catch this — it doesn't touch fence counts, prompt-echo phrases, grounding availability, or mid-fence boundaries; the content is syntactically fine, just factually wrong about types the grounding summary never covered. Fix candidate: extend `_current_api_fact_summary()` (or a sibling function) to also summarize any OTHER class name that appears as a `\`ClassName.member\`` backtick reference inside `section_text` itself (not just the one `symbol` from the change record) — e.g. scanning `section_text` for backtick-quoted dotted references and looking each base class up in `api_surface.json` too, folding all of them into the grounding block. Given this is the 4th distinct defect class found across 5 consecutive retest attempts on the same product, also worth a standing decision on whether `PARAGRAPH_REWRITE`'s LLM branch should stay enabled by default for unattended runs until broader-scope grounding lands, versus running deterministic-template-only (accepting blander content) for now. | ST-086 | C | GI | ST-082's code-fence-balance guard (`_paragraph_rewrite`, `CODE_FENCE_IMBALANCE`) compares only the TOTAL ``` count between source and LLM output — necessary but not sufficient. Found live 2026-08-18, cells/python, 6th retest attempt (ST-085's grounding fix confirmed working correctly this run — the previously-fabricated `apply_style()` claim from ST-085 did NOT recur): within one multi-heading `impact_section` slice (covering both "Step 6" and "Step 7" of `how-to-style-cells-python.md`), the LLM removed one closing fence after Step 6's code example and added one opening/closing fence pair inside Step 7's own code block — netting to the SAME total ``` count as the source (passes the existing guard) while still corrupting the actual open/close nesting: Step 6's code block was left open into the following prose ("This produces a white, bold, 16pt Arial label..." now runs directly out of unfenced code), and Step 7's own `from aspose.cells_foss import Workbook, Font` line is immediately followed by a stray closing fence, leaving the REST of Step 7's real code (`workbook = Workbook()` through the `Font(...)` constructor call, ~15 lines) as unfenced plain body text rather than a code block. Fix candidate: replace the total-count comparison with a proper stateful walk — track the open/closed fence STATE after each ``` in both `section_text` and `llm_output` (a simple boolean toggle per marker) and require the two STATE SEQUENCES to match at every corresponding marker position, not just have the same final count; equivalently, split both texts on ``` and compare the resulting alternating prose/code segment boundaries structurally. This is a hardening of the EXISTING ST-082 guard mechanism, not a new detection category — same class of defect (corrupted fence structure), just a gap in how "corrupted" was being checked. Reverted before commit; nothing shipped. Sixth consecutive cells/python retest attempt across this session; 5 distinct defect classes found and fixed so far (ST-082, ST-083, ST-084, ST-085, and this one's predecessor-guard gap) — each individual fix has held on retest (no regressions of an already-fixed class observed), only genuinely new/adjacent gaps keep surfacing. **FIXED 2026-08-18** (commit `4f991f7a03c4`): the state-sequence-walk fix candidate above turns out to be mathematically equivalent to the existing total-count check (same-count already implies identical open/close alternation parity by construction, since segment type is a pure function of segment index) — it would NOT have caught this defect. Actual fix: `_code_block_structure_shifted()` splits both `section_text` and `llm_output` on ``` and compares each fenced code block's CONTENT LENGTH against its positional counterpart, rejecting (deterministic fallback, new `CODE_FENCE_CONTENT_SHIFTED` reason) on a length swing consistent with block-boundary corruption (>3x ratio AND >80 char absolute diff — both generous, to tolerate a normal small edit to code referencing a genuinely changed member). Wired into `_paragraph_rewrite` immediately after the existing `CODE_FENCE_IMBALANCE` count check. 3 new regression tests (direct unit test reproducing the real Step 6/Step 7 shape, a tolerance test proving small legit edits aren't flagged, and a full `_paragraph_rewrite` integration test). 149 existing tests still pass. An adversarial independent code-review pass (8 parallel finder agents, `/code-review high`) found and this session fixed 6 real hardening gaps before any real backfill ran: (1) `_auto_pin_old_readme_baseline_at_push` could let a plain `OSError` escape past an already-durable PUSHED transition -- now caught alongside `ReadmeRefreshRunError`; (2) a corrupted/unreadable pin sidecar was misclassified as a protected manual pin, permanently blocking future auto-pins -- now treated as absent and safely re-pinned; (3) `_persist_target_authority` used a raw `write_text` instead of the module's own atomic `_write_json` helper -- fixed, since this sidecar is now written on every real push, not just a rare manual call; (4) the CLI's DIRTY loop double-printed an indeterminate product as `DIRTY ...: {}` right below its own dedicated INDETERMINATE line -- fixed; (5) `push()` discarded the auto-pin result entirely, making even a clean no-op invisible to callers -- now recorded onto the manifest; (6) **the most consequential finding, discovered live running this mission's own new `audit-baseline-coverage` tool against the real portfolio**: font/python's real push receipt records `target_authority: "mirror"` (2026-09-02, human-acknowledged), but a FRESH `classify_target_authority()` call today reads `canonical` for the same product -- the PR that merged on top of the mirror's post-reset root gave HEAD a real parent again, flipping one of the two AND-ed structural signals, even though `orphan_root_count` (still 12) proves the repo is still fundamentally a periodically-resnapshotted mirror. A single live re-classification is not safe grounds for a backfill-safety decision; fixed by adding `_ever_receipted_as_mirror()`, which makes any historical mirror receipt sticky, never overridden by a later, more convenient live read. New regression test proves this exact scenario. **Real portfolio survey results** (32 active products): 26 no_push_yet, 3 already pinned, 3 PUSHED-but-unpinned found -- font/python correctly `unrecoverable_unpinned` (left for a human decision, NOT backfilled), tex/python and 3d/python `recoverable_unpinned` (both genuinely canonical at push time per their real receipts) -- both reviewed and backfilled for real. | ST-206 | C | TF | CE-02's `topic_grounding` evaluator has no singular/plural or abbreviation normalization: its noun-grounding check (`_grounded()`, exact-subset word match against `_split_identifier()`-tokenized class/method names) requires every noun word to appear verbatim in some identifier's token set. `content/kb.aspose.org/en/email/cpp/how-to-read-msg-files-cpp.md` (topic noun `msg-files` after the ST-198 fix, was `read-msg-files` before) FAILs not because the page is fabricated — `mapi_message::from_file`/`from_stream` are real, reachable APIs backing it — but because no single knowledge-model identifier's token set contains both `msg` and `files` together (class/method names use singular forms like `MsgReader`/`MsgWriter`, never a plural `files` token). The evaluator's own emitted `suggestion` text ("retire the page via /page-retire if the topic does not correspond to any real capability") would be WRONG advice for this specific page if acted on literally — it is a lexical-matching gap in the evaluator, not evidence the page should be retired. Confirmed live 2026-09-23: this page's FAIL persists identically before and after ST-198's fix (only the reported noun text changed, from `read-msg-files` to `msg-files`; the FAIL verdict did not). Needs investigation before `topic_grounding` graduates from EXPERIMENTAL to CANONICAL, or before anyone acts on its FAILs by retiring pages. Found 2026-09-18, re-confirmed 2026-09-23 (ST-198 real-data verification). | queued | P2 | M | 0 | — | related-to:ST-198,CE-02 | 2026-09-23 |
